## 2026-08-16 — the live admission proof a session cannot make, archived from STATE at the 2026-08-18 drift audit

*(The author drove the join-code half of the live admission flow end to end in a real
browser with a second Google account. STATE keeps the one-paragraph result and the list of
what it does NOT cover, because both still gate what a session may claim; this is the full
record.)*

**✅ THE ONE PROOF NO SESSION CAN MAKE — COLLECTED 2026-08-16 (author, real browser, second Google account).** The join-code half of the live admission flow has now been driven end to end by a human: a pre-existing teacher account used class `7NE9M2` (created 2026-08-14 — no class was made that day), and a second, distinct Google account joined it as a student. **Tool-read confirmation, not a claim-read:** `users` went 3 teachers / **0 students → 3 teachers / 1 student** (row created `2026-08-16 00:28:27+00`), `7NE9M2` shows **1 active member**, and **no `pending` rows remain** (consistent with promotion completing — `pending` is transient). The class's `expected_domain` is **null** and no `student_domain` row exists, so that account matched **no** fast path. That discharges all three items this block used to name: **the OAuth round trip** (the dashboard redirect allowlist covers the flow), **the trigger admitting a stranger**, and **`retryRole` after promotion**.

**What the collected proof does NOT cover — read this before citing it:**
- **Gate 4 is untouched.** What ran was `redeem_join_code`. The **`student_domain` fast path has still never executed live** — no seed, no exercise. It stays deliberately last (see its own item below).
- **`claim_teacher` has never promoted anyone live.** All 3 teacher rows predate the admission slice (newest 2026-07-29), so the "I'm a teacher" self-serve door — the one D24 is actually about — is still unexercised by a stranger.
- **✅ Confirmed by the author on the same run:** the brief neutral gate card DID appear between "Joining class…" and "You're in ✓", and read as normal loading rather than an error. Expected and correct — `retryRole()` sets `roleStatus='loading'` in the same batch as the success state. The question this closes is not "does it render" but "does a real person misread it as a failure"; they did not. Leave the sequence alone.

*(Repro, if it is ever needed again: `/` → type `7NE9M2` → Continue with Google → expect "You're in ✓" then Algebra 1 on Home.)* Proven around it beforehand, and still valid: verify-0033 10/10, the integration lane through the real trigger, RTL, and the pre-auth half against the LIVE meta endpoint in a real browser (bad code → the DR-6 warning; `7NE9M2` → "Algebra 1" in the title slot).

## 2026-08-17 — the 0036 / arming-arc session close, archived from STATE at the 2026-08-18 drift audit

*(Moved verbatim from STATE.md's "Last updated" block. Its live residue — the rollup's
ruled design, verify-0035's designed P5 flip, and the arming checklist — already lives in
STATE's check-prune section and the TODOS arming-arc entry; this is the narrative only.)*

*(Prior entry, 2026-08-17 — 0036 applied live, 147/0, pushed, CI-green at 31999243137.)* **The months-parked pruning/rollup entry went from parked to fully built in one day, across two arcs and two eng reviews.** Morning: re-derivation (P10) → plan → review → **the outside voice overturned the ratified frame** ("build the rollup now" fell to "prune disarmed now, rollup at arming") → 0035 shipped and applied. Evening: the author pulled the arming arc forward, and Part II went plan → review → build → 0036.

**The Part II review caught its OWN new ruling — the pattern worth keeping.** The eng pass ruled a zone-change self-heal (a rolled day's key is frozen, so changing zones double-counts boundary hours). The outside voice then found that the self-heal's rebuild recomputes from RAW rows, which the armed prune deletes: an unclamped rebuild would silently collapse months of `*_all` history. Fix: every recompute path is horizon-clamped to days the prune guarantees raw-complete, older days freeze under their recorded zone, and a deep rebuild is honored with a warning. **Two reviews, two same-day rulings overturned by the outside voice** — it keeps paying for itself.

**Four defects the matrix caught before 0036 could apply, and only one was in the test.** (1) A live bug: the roll passed a bare `array_agg` to a helper where NULL means "all versions", so a quiet night silently recomputed every version in the database — **and that accidental recompute was masking the very drift the reconciliation row exists to observe**. (2) A compile error: `v_versions` already names 0026's census count in the same function. (3+4) Two of my own assertions were wrong rather than the code (`correct_all` arithmetic; a global ledger sum compared against a fixture P2 had deliberately polluted) — the reconciliation row was rewritten to assert a DELTA against a baseline, which is also the honest post-arming reading.

**The P5 flips landed where the retired guards said they would.** verify-0035's `rolled_through_never_written` became a scoping assertion, and its §I **inverted**: the fixture is rolled before the prune, so `*_all` is UNCHANGED across it. That delta of zero, asserted exactly where the honest loss used to be, is the arc's promise made checkable.

**What a future session needs from this arc:** (1) the rollup's design is fully ruled and recorded (plan §5 + the TODOS arming-arc entry) — inherit, don't re-derive; among the rulings is a per-teacher timezone because **the platform now spans the US and New Zealand** (author's own answer — a fact no doc previously recorded). (2) verify-0035 §A's `rolled_through_never_written` row is designed to go red when the arming arc lands — flip it there (P5). (3) The review found two pre-existing drift errors in retention-policy.md (deletion order citing dropped `grades`; stale version header) — both fixed at draft-5. (4) The build's one new mechanical lesson: two ledger rows written in one transaction tie on `ran_at` (transaction-constant `now()` — 0034's graded_at lesson, third sighting), so the watermark read orders by `id desc`.

## 2026-08-16 (late) — teacher-grading session close, archived from STATE at the 0035 session

*(Moved verbatim from STATE.md's "Last updated" block when the 0035 slice replaced it.)*

**The reviews changed the architecture, which is the argument for running them before building.** The eng outside voice's 14 findings produced four rulings that are now load-bearing code: writes gate on `can_edit_activity` (immune to the recorded Activity-Bank read-widening landmine), maxPoints is denormalized server-side so the student read never opens the raw document, `graded_by` is SET NULL rather than RESTRICT, and the pruning follow-on inherited an explicit "never delete a graded check row" constraint. The design outside voice then **overturned an eng layout call** — version headers were the wrong level-1 for a grading queue — and its two critical findings (a released grade must SAY it is live-edited; release needed a student-perceivable signal) both shipped.

**Four defects the tests caught before shipping, each a different class, and the reason to keep writing the matrix first:**
1. **Dependency order** — dropping `can_grade_submission` before `grades` fails; the table's policies depend on it (SQLSTATE 2BP01).
2. **A live cron that would have died at its next fire, not at migration time** — `purge_soft_deleted` cites `grades`. Rewritten without the blocker, which is a semantic change (SET NULL supersedes the RESTRICT that required it), not a port.
3. **Nondeterminism in the staleness winner** — verify-0034 §D failed on the identical-text row, the one the whole G2 ruling exists for. `graded_at` defaults to `now()`, the TRANSACTION timestamp, so two grades written together tie and the `id desc` tiebreaker compared random uuids. Both functions now break ties on attempt_number.
4. **Vacuous coverage, caught in the act** — the three new integration rows SKIPPED on first run because the lane's fixture had no written-answer block. Fixed the fixture, not the assertion.

**Two operational facts** (both since promoted into STATE's standing notes): local verify reds are seeded-data preconditions whose exact pair depends on local data state, and a push can silently not land — `git ls-remote origin refs/heads/main` is the only check that settles it.

*(Prior entry:)* **The admission side quest closed by finding what the slice had missed** — R5-DR's ruled PRE-AUTH fork existed only as its post-auth safety net, while `Home.tsx` carried a comment asserting the fork routed users. Shipped as T7 with the P5 flip on the e2e row that pinned the replaced heading. The counsel packet states the draft-2 → draft-3 delta and asks nine questions; writing it caught `data-map.md` claiming migrations 0001–0027 while documenting 0033's columns.

## 2026-08-14 — the integration lane's first run: the database could not be rebuilt

Drop 5's author half finally ran (Docker Desktop installed on the previously Docker-less machine), and the lane found **three real defects on its first honest run** — including one that had made the repo's migration chain unreplayable for months without leaving a trace on live.

**1. The chain aborted at 0009.** `revoke execute on function rls_auto_enable()` — a function 0009's own comment documents as *"Created via the dashboard, not by a migration — recorded here for provenance."* It is not provenance; it is an executable dependency on a dashboard-only object. Live has the function, so 0009 applied cleanly long ago and the defect was **invisible from production**. A fresh replay halted there, which meant: no environment could be built from migrations, disaster recovery from migrations was broken, and the integration lane could never have passed on **any** machine, because its preflight runs `supabase db reset` on every run. Docker was never the only thing in the way.

The lesson is sharper than the bug. DECISIONS.md had recorded the provenance explicitly, *"so the next catalog-vs-repo diff doesn't treat it as a mystery"* — and the note worked perfectly as documentation while doing nothing to prevent the executable break. **Provenance in a comment is not reproducibility.** The fix: 0009's revoke is existence-guarded, and **0031** recreates the function + `ensure_rls` event trigger, written so live is a strict no-op (creates only when ABSENT; deliberately not `create or replace`, so live's working definition is never overwritten by the reconstruction). ⚠ The body IS a reconstruction — nothing in the repo ever recorded the original — and the file carries the query to diff it against live.

**2. The migrations never granted the client roles at all.** Every table created before 0028 received its anon/authenticated/service_role DML from **Supabase's hosted platform defaults**, never from a migration. The local CLI stack (2.109.0) does not reproduce them — its `public` default ACL is `postgres=arwdDxtm | service_role=Dxtm`, client roles absent entirely. Result on a rebuilt database: **10 of 11 policy-bearing tables had RLS policies that could never be satisfied**, which is precisely the dependency 0028's own header states — a policy can only permit what a grant already allows.

**0032** encodes them, **read off live** (`supabase db query --linked`) rather than reasoned about; the rebuilt grant matrix now diffs **IDENTICAL** to live. It deliberately reproduces live's full breadth rather than a narrower "safer" set, and the reasoning is worth keeping: **a local database stricter than production MASKS RLS defects.** A policy hole would be invisible locally (the missing grant refuses first) and open in production — a test environment stricter than production manufactures false confidence about exactly the layer that separates student data. Fidelity is the security property. The seven zero-policy tables and `classes`' 0027 narrowing (no table-level INSERT/UPDATE; column-level UPDATE(name) only) are preserved exactly.

**3. Three of the lane's own assertions were stubs.** UI copy retyped from imagination into a lane that had never executed, so nothing could contradict it: the teacher link is `My activities`, not `Activities`; the join-success copy is `&apos;` (U+0027) while the assertion used a curly U+2019 — two strings that can never match and look identical in a diff; and seeding went through PostgREST as `service_role`, which holds no DML on `student_domain`/`allowlist` locally. Seeding now runs as `postgres` via the CLI, matching the path 0013 documents (*"the author seeds rows in the SQL editor"*), which also removes any dependence on grant shape — **not** by granting service_role DML, since those two tables are the admission boundary 0027/0028 hardened and widening a production grant to serve a test is the wrong direction. Per the same day's tab-lock lesson, all three were audited and fixed together rather than one per run.

**Correction recorded the same day:** the claim "the lane's seeding was never viable on any machine" was too strong. Live *does* hold `service_role=FULL` on both tables, so the original PostgREST path would have worked there — it was a local/live divergence, not a universally broken path.

**Result: 3/3 GREEN**, the lane's first green run — real users through the real `handle_new_auth_user` trigger (including the refused outsider), a real `join_class` via `/join/:code`, real publish + share, and one genuine `check-activity` round trip. Plain sweep still excludes it (304 tests, 0 integration).

**Two environment traps found while verifying, both in TODOS:** the `sw` lane's offline-reopen rows fail while the local Supabase stack is running (stop it before a plain sweep), and heavy background load — a game client at ~80% CPU plus Docker — turned a 40-second four-lane run into 14.5 minutes and failed five `failure-matrix` rows that are green on a quiet machine, including rows nobody had touched. **Local e2e timing is not trustworthy under load; CI is the arbiter.**

## 2026-08-14 — the S9 cutover arc: all five drops, repo-side (archived from STATE 2026-08-14)

The whole arc landed in one day. Drop order was the eng review's (OV-3), not the plan's original: **1 publish-rewrite → 3 demolition → 4 renderer-death → 2 content-surface → 5 proof-lanes**. Author stations ran alongside; Drops 1 and 3 closed end to end the same day.

**Drop 1 — publish rewrite (T1).** Publish became a direct `publish_activity` RPC from `usePublish`, no Edge Function: the autosave flush ABORTS publish on failure (`useAutosave.flush` now reports success honestly, OV-2), `ActivityDocument.safeParse` runs pre-publish on the exact persisted payload via the editor's `lastPersistedDraft` mirror (E-1), PostgREST raises map to teacher copy (`mapPublishRpcError`), and `PublishStatus` (extracted to `components/PublishStatus.tsx`) builds the share link as the viewer URL. Deleted: `VITE_PUBLISHED_URL_BASE` (+ example env + vite-env typing), `publishedUrl()`, the unconsumed `versioned_url`, `deploy:publish` (OV-DX-2). Tests: 12 usePublish unit rows, 4 PublishStatus RTL, 3 e2e rows. **No census write anywhere in the new path** (the S7 amendment holds by construction). **Station: pushed (CI run 31765685636 green), HEAD recorded `27a4a08dd7b58aa460f5c13d9d360d85f0ec13df`, `publish-activity` DELETED from the platform.** Two things surfaced at the station and were fixed rather than noted: `config.toml` still carried a `publish-activity` flag entry (a deploy-all resurrection path the script deletion didn't close), and the "Baseline facts" version numbers were all one low with a "verified live" stamp they hadn't earned.

> **Correction kept for the record (P11):** STATE long read "verified live 2026-08-07 … through 0026; ingest v42 / get-feedback v4 / get-activity v9 / check-activity v6". Every version was **exactly one low**, and `ingest-submission` proves those numbers were never live-read as claimed: its `updated_at` was still 2026-07-29, so a function nobody redeployed cannot have gained a version. Three of the four stale numbers match the `entrypoint_path` suffix `list_edge_functions` also returns (`…_42`, `…_9`, `…_6`) — the likely misread, a plausible-looking field one line from the right one. **The flags, which are the part that decides whether a student reaches their first screen, were correct throughout** — the risk was a false "verified live" stamp, not a live misconfiguration.

**Drop 3 — demolition (T3+T4), hard-gated on zero-traffic evidence.** Migration **0029**: counted wipe (P7), `ingest_submission` RPC dropped, `submissions.student_id` branch demolished (column + FK + both partial indexes, CHECK back to 2 branches) — **plus a P10 find the plan missed: `purge_soft_deleted` (live, cron-scheduled nightly) read `submissions.student_id` in two guards and is re-created without them in 0029; unhandled, the nightly purge would have died at its first post-apply fire.** The whole migration was rehearsed against the live DB in a rolled-back transaction: end to end, real 17-row wipe, a live run of the recreated purge, 11/11 verify assertions green, world confirmed untouched. **Phase 2.6 manual grading RETIRED (OV-5):** Submissions route + grading.tsx + lib/submissions + lib/grades + 3 test files deleted; entry links removed (no dead links); README's add-a-block-type ★ step tombstoned; StudentViewer's dormant feedbackUrl tombstoned. **Compliance:** the ip_hash row CLOSED by data removal, data-map updated, the GAP-3 pin flipped to guard the closure disclosure (P11).

> **📌 OV-10 zero-traffic evidence (captured 2026-08-14 at Drop 3 start — the durable record):** (a) newest `submissions` row **2026-07-29 12:34:25 UTC** — nothing in 16 days; 17 rows total (6 with ip_hash, 0 with student_id), `grades` 0 rows, `section_checks` 0 rows. (b) 24-hour unified-log window (`query_logs` over `edge_logs`, grouped by `request.path`): **zero requests to `/functions/v1/ingest-submission` or `/functions/v1/get-feedback`** — the busiest paths were `/auth/v1/callback` and `/auth/v1/authorize` at 8 requests each (Station 0's own probe traffic). Both halves of OV-10's definition met.

> **📌 OV-6 P9 audit:** the status table's 2026-07-13 "live-verified" claim for Phase 2.6 was **FALSE for the student half and provably never true** — `get-feedback` served bodiless 200s from birth. Corrections recorded in the 07-14 headline and the Drop-0 CORS deferral (the predicted sibling instance). **The parked teacher-grading slice must not treat get-feedback as a reference implementation.**

**Station: CLOSED end to end** — CI green (run 31772779555, 4m43s; one student spec passed on retry, the failure-matrix tab-lock row); 0029 applied; `verify:auth --target live` all PASS; wipe confirmed live (submissions 0 / grades 0), `student_id` column gone, `ingest_submission` RPC gone, `purge_soft_deleted` recreated without the doomed guards; **`ingest-submission` + `get-feedback` DELETED**. HEAD recorded `63db47659d7631f04ba9e9da1b6e8f59cc78be04`.

**Drop 4 — renderer death + doc migration (T5).** `packages/renderer` deleted whole (726 tests, the runtime, RUNTIME.md, the A9 bond, the grading-parity file — all self-declared S9 deaths; the corpus survives viewer-side as the standing gate). With it: `bundle-renderer.mjs` + the `bundle:renderer` script + the CI renderer-drift step (2 bundle guards remain), `RUNTIME_SIZE_TARGET/CEILING` + their pins, `_shared/renderer.bundle.js` + `graph-kit-manifest.ts`, and the three deleted functions' source dirs — `supabase/functions/` now holds exactly get-activity + check-activity, matching the platform. **Cache pair REMOVED (D-8/C6):** `purgeStudentCaches` + `sweepForeignCaches` + the signOutEverything call site + the C6 allowlist entry; re-triage kept only `VIEWER_SHELL_CACHE` (live consumer: main.tsx warmAssetCache); the S6-6 purge-ORDER pin updated in place, not deleted. **13-target claims-grep + doc migration done** across CLAUDE.md, README, ROADMAP, corpus.ts, printChecks.ts and STATE.

Rehearsed live in a rolled-back transaction: the full verify-0030 §B matrix green (4/4 refusals, audit=2 with no phantom rows, dedupe no-op, member-sees / outsider-sees-nothing scope proof, soft-delete filters, normalized+gated meta), residue zero. **T7:** get-activity gains the anonymous `join_code` meta branch — same limiter (ONE window across both anon lookups, P3 liveness row fires it at production values), wire-leak row (class name and NOTHING else), 6 new handler rows (43 total). **T8, from the v2 board verbatim:** the Classes card's "On students' Home" disclosure (DR-1 mirror ordering, DR-3 committed counts, DR-5 confirmed remove + honest undo failure with pause-on-hover/focus + focus handoff, DR-7 muted dead rows, DR-9 picker states incl. distinct none-published / all-added), the editor's post-publish "Add to a class…" hook (DR-8 stays-open multi-period, DR-12 44px + dot+ink success, DR-13 one-class pre-select), the student Home nested list (DR-9d headers always render, per-class empty is THE empty state, ONE un-indented status line with its own selector, whole-row 44px links), and the join gate's ONE composition (DR-4 reserved 2-line title slot — the name replaces text, never structure; DR-6 pre-OAuth definitive-negative warning, silent network fallback). **T16:** the generic sign-in body stops guessing a cause. Entry chunk 174.0/185 KiB gz — the whole student content surface cost 1.0 KiB.

**Station:** 0030 applied live; first `verify:auth --target live` run went 84/1 — verify-0017 §D still said "exactly one anon-reachable function" while its twin (verify-0028 §A) had moved to two with 0030. **The twin-drift class, caught by the check itself** and fixed in `3b69ed5` (both copies now assert the roster BY NAME and cite each other). Not an exposure — the second anon RPC is 0030's design. Re-run: **85 passed / 0 failed across 8 scripts.** `get-activity` deployed to v11, `verify_jwt:false` preserved; the join_code meta branch live-probed correct.

**Drop 5 — proof lanes (T9+T10+T11).** **T9 found a real product bug:** the in-test server-stop harness (`e2e/helpers/disposablePreview.ts` — a preview server killed mid-test) reproduced the parked offline-reopen failure against a genuinely dead server, and the diagnosis showed `Vary: Origin` on cached assets defeating `Cache.match` — **offline reopen had never worked for anyone; TV2-A's promise was broken in the shipped worker.** Fixed (`matchOptions: ignoreVary` — the hash IS the identity), plus two harness lessons encoded in-file (a routed page blocks SW navigations even after unrouteAll → the reopen uses a FRESH page, which is also the honest story; a fill racing navigation loses the debounced buffer → the kill waits for the buffer to provably hold the work). Both rows un-parked, 7/7 ×3. **T10:** the `a11y` lane — four real-browser gap rows + axe per surface; **axe's first catch was real** (callout-note 4.39:1 AA failure → token fix to 6.87:1, and the whole callout family joined the tokens contrast guard). **T11:** the `integration` lane — `supabase db reset` per run, email+password users through the REAL trigger (incl. the refused outsider), real join_class via /join/:code, real publish + 0030 share + list, and one REAL check-activity round trip; gated behind `test:e2e:integration` (never pollutes a plain sweep — verified 302/0), preflight-with-named-fixes PROVEN on a Docker-less machine (D7).

**The a11y lane's first two CI catches, and the lesson underneath them (2026-08-14, post-arc).** The lane went red on its first real CI run and the fix went red again — worth keeping because the second failure was the same mistake as the first.

1. **`aria-prohibited-attr`** on the display-mode graph/chart divs: `aria-label` on a role-less `<div>`. The kit patched `role=img` on only after the lazy chunk mounted, so first paint was invalid. Fixed in `227af90` — display mode sets `role=img` in JSX.
2. **The scan itself was vacuous, twice (P9).** `227af90` also added a "wait for the kit to mount" step so local and CI would scan the same DOM — but **the lazy tier renders nothing at all, markers included, until its chunk resolves**, so the `if (canvases.count() > 0)` guard counted an element that could not exist yet and skipped the wait entirely. Probed at scan time: 0 canvases, 0 math-fields, 0 prompt hosts. Local scanned pre-mount and passed; CI scanned mounted and stayed red. **The fix for a vacuous scan had shipped its own vacuity.** Now derived from the registry's lazy tier + the served fixture (P2), with a guard row asserting every lazy binding declares a mount marker (P11).
3. **`aria-input-field-name` — a real defect the vacuous scan had been hiding.** MathLive gives its keyboard sink `role="textbox"` and no accessible name, so a screen reader announced a student's math answer box as an unlabeled textbox. 0.109.2 offers **no API** for it (`ariaLabel` in its types is for menu items; an `aria-label` on the host does not reach the sink — both verified against the running component). Fixed by naming the shadow node in `mountMathPrompts`; CI is the guard if a MathLive rename breaks it.
4. **`nested-interactive` — carved out, with a self-retiring guard.** MathLive's own structure (focusable host wrapping a focusable sink); not reachable from our code (host `tabindex=-1` does not clear it). Excluded by rule AND element with reason + owner in-file, plus a row asserting the carve-out is **still load-bearing**, so it fails and demands deletion at the next MathLive major rather than outliving its reason.
5. **The tab-lock row's second sighting.** Settling passes through "exactly one tab enabled" more than once (StrictMode remounts both tabs), so snapshotting the winner at the first sample and then filling it raced — on a slow runner the lock had moved and `fill()` sat retrying against a disabled input until timeout, which is exactly the CI log in both failures. Now requires two consecutive agreeing samples. **Not reproduced locally** (8 isolated runs stayed green); the fix is reasoned from the CI log.

Recorded, not fixed (author-ruled): the mounted worksheet puts **Check 76 tab stops in**, ~17 of them canvas handles — all named, so axe is clean; it is a UX question, and TODOS carries the measurement and the design question.

## 2026-08-04 → 08-05 — S7 closed + S8 built (archived from STATE 2026-08-07)

**S7 CLOSED and S8 BUILT** (commits `e3fbcf3`, `0e0c888`; both pushed). S7's remaining author steps all landed: 0026 applied and verified live, `get-activity` v8 deployed, the census backfill run (24 versions censused, 110 correctly skipped as unservable `schemaVersion 1`), and a live e2e probe 8/0. **The one thing still open across both slices is observing the cron's first fire** — prediction and query at the top of this file.

**S8 in one line: the entry chunk went 2.86 MB → 592 KB raw (168.1 KiB gz), and CI now guards it.** Students no longer download Tiptap, the image cropper, or any authoring surface. Twelve deterministic size budgets run on every PR, 23 `node --test` cases exercise the budget script's own failure paths (including the vacuity guards, which are unreachable from a healthy build), a throttled perf lane measures `performance.mark('student-interactive:*')` on the production build, and the sw + student e2e lanes finally run in CI — they were local-only, which made "keep them green" an aspiration rather than a gate, and route-splitting is exactly the change that breaks stale-chunk recovery.

**Three things worth carrying forward.** (1) **The eng review's own smoke test was vacuous and the outside voice caught it:** every teacher route sits behind `RequireAuth`, so an unauthenticated route-mount smoke passes on six completely broken chunks. (2) **Measurement can lie confidently** — CDP throttling is not reliably applied through service-worker-mediated fetches, so the lane asserts a fresh context and `workerStart === 0` or the numbers are fiction. (3) **The KaTeX question was a false binary.** Reframed to three options, the winner was *preload-on-math-detect*: fire the fetch when the document is known to contain math rather than when a component mounts. That halved the LaTeX-fallback window (~737 → ~382 ms) for a ~40 ms cost to worksheet-interactive, with no shell growth and no D16 amendment — where eager loading would have cost +45% first load on every math page.

**Five defects were found by running things rather than reasoning about them,** all in test/fixture code: an ambiguous `Loading…` selector that couldn't distinguish a route's own loading state from the Suspense fallback; `.viewer-section` being rendered by the loading skeleton too, so a timing spec could read a mark before the worksheet existed and blame the instrumentation; a periodic fixture filler gzip crushed, so three over-cap cases silently landed under cap; and the student path fetching pwa-register/workbox chunks off the render path. Suite +36 (viewer 1058, app 1035) plus the new 23 script tests and 11 perf specs.

**Prior entry, 2026-08-04 (later session):** **S7 analytics census BUILT.**  **five migrations, all applied and verified: 0021 (display_name privacy), 0022 (purge-job section_checks fix), 0023 (account retention clock), 0024 (audit actor on purge), 0025 (derived student dormancy).** A privacy fix opened the thread; refreshing the compliance pack to match it exposed that the retention machinery underneath had never worked — the nightly purge would have died once checks aged in, it deleted `public.users` while leaving the Google identity behind, and no account could be purged at all. All three are fixed, and account deletion now demonstrably completes end to end (proven against the live DB in rolled-back transactions, the discipline used for every fix in this arc). 0025 closes the arc by starting the clock, and changed its substance as well as its mechanism: the account window went from 30 days to 400, because 30 is shorter than a summer break and would have purged students over the holidays. Retention now stands complete; the standing note under Standing constraints records the one invariant not to break. Trigger stops storing emails, anon meta RPC refuses to return them, 2 rows backfilled to NULL; name-appearance control backlogged. Full verification pass green including a live anonymous HTTP fetch (detail under Pending author actions). One decision left open there: a third account still publishes the real name "Zan Reed" — intended behavior, but worth an explicit call. Earlier same day: drift audit + STATE restructure (9 mechanical findings fixed, finished narratives to [HISTORY.md](docs/HISTORY.md)); suite verified green (counts above).

## 2026-08-03 → 08-04 — components-as-data S5.5 (teacher print migration, T1–T11) + the never-identity shuffle deploy (archived from STATE 2026-08-04)

S5.5 closed 2026-08-03: the teacher print path — worksheet and foldable — runs entirely on the viewer tree; `@activity/renderer` has no consumer in `packages/app` product code and survives only for published pages until S9. Rulings: gstack design doc → "S5.5 eng review" (23 decisions). The build narrative, archived from STATE's Current focus:

**⚠ SEQUENCING NOTE — T8 LANDED BEFORE T7, at the author's explicit direction ("do T8 now, I'll handle the baselines after"), and D9A's constraint is still satisfied IN SUBSTANCE.** That ruling gated eviction on the two legs for one reason: the contact sheet compares the renderer against the viewer, so evicting first would destroy the thing being signed off. The eviction was therefore scoped to **product code only** — `packages/app/e2e` keeps importing `@activity/renderer` deliberately, so the contact sheet, the parity gate and the answer-key gate all still run. **Both legs remain fully doable; nothing about them got harder.** What is now true that wasn't: a re-import into `src/` fails lint.

**T7 — both legs done.**

1. **✅ CONTACT SHEET SIGNED OFF — the author read it and accepted the result (2026-08-03).** This is the third leg of the S5-6 parity artifact and the half no test can supply: 22 rows, renderer beside viewer, both in print media. **This was the last moment it could ever be read** — the comparison exists only while the renderer is still reachable — so with it recorded, the human-judged half of print parity is complete for S5.5. ~~Regenerate any time with `PRINT_CONTACT_SHEET=1 pnpm ... playwright test print-contact-sheet`~~ **⚠ CORRECTED 2026-08-13 (S9 recon, P5-class stale claim): the sheet is UNREGENERABLE** — the generator spec was deleted with the cross-surface gates (`29ea4f5`, below) and the renderer is eslint-banned and undependenced from packages/app, so this command matches no spec and cannot ever run again. **The durable evidence is the B15 archive: annotated tag `s5.5-print-signoff` + the GitHub release asset `s5.5-contact-sheet.zip`** (44 PNGs + index.html; the B5 touch-target height fix postdates the sign-off — the tag message says so).

2. **✅ LINUX PRINT BASELINES DONE (`4743c66`) — 22 images committed AND CI now compares them.** Committing the images alone would have left them inert: the suite skips without `PRINT_BASELINES`, which the routine job never set, so nothing compared anything. The `print gates` job now sets it, in COMPARE mode — the absence of `--update-snapshots` is the only thing separating it from the generation job. Local runs still skip, because macOS rasterisation fails against Linux images for no reason a human would call a defect (S5-7).

   **They were generated THREE times, and the two discarded sets paid for themselves.** Run 1 pinned a BLANK `math_block`: the harness emulated print MEDIA but never set the component's print MODE, and in that hybrid MathLive mounts, the component hides the KaTeX behind it, and the print stylesheet hides the field — so both vanish. Fixing that surfaced **a KaTeX error that had been printing on real worksheets since S3**: substituting a bare `\square` into `\placeholder[g1]{}x` glues into `\squarex`, one unknown command, rendered as red error text. Fixing THAT surfaced a specificity bug in S5.5's own preview CSS — `[data-viewer-mode='print'] .thing` (0,2,0) outranked the print block's plain class (0,1,0) and won *inside* `@media print`, removing the gap between "Name" and "Date" on paper. Preview selectors are now wrapped in `:where()` so they cannot outrank print by construction. **None of the three was reachable by a rules assertion** — they are about how something looks, not whether an element exists, which is the gap screenshots exist to cover.

   **Refreshing them:** `workflow_dispatch` → `update_print_baselines`, download, replace, commit. Start a FRESH dispatch rather than re-running an old run — a re-run replays its ORIGINAL commit, which cost a cycle here.

**✅ DONE (`29ea4f5`) — the cross-surface gates are retired (S5-abs).** The comparison had done its job, and it could never have been deferred: it needed the renderer reachable. The renderer half of the rules and answer-key specs is gone, along with the contact-sheet generator; **the coverage stayed** — the four answer-key comparisons became viewer-only pins with the extracted key as the oracle. The contract collapsed with them: `target` was a per-surface map with a `notApplicable` escape that existed so a rule could apply to the viewer and not the renderer, and with one surface there is nothing to except a rule from (per-block exemptions already live in `suppressedChecksFor`), so the map, the escape, `PrintSurface` and `IMPROVED_BREAK_INSIDE` all went. **Renamed, because "parity" on a single-surface gate misleads the next reader:** `print-parity.e2e.ts` → `print-rules.e2e.ts`, `printParity.ts` → `printChecks.ts`, CI job → `print gates`; each header records what it used to be so the history does not read as a deletion. **`@activity/renderer` is now out of `packages/app` entirely** — not a dependency, not a devDependency — and the lint guard covers the whole package rather than just `src/`.

- **T8 (`1319130`) — the renderer is out of product code.** Zero imports in `packages/app/src` and `packages/viewer/src`, and the BUILT app carries none of its output (renderer-only markers absent from `dist`, viewer equivalents present). It is now a devDependency of the app, which is the honest description: a test-only dependency of the cross-surface gates. **D18A found real drift** — the id→family map existed in THREE copies (renderer `FONT_REGISTRY`, viewer `CSS_FAMILY`, app reading the renderer's), and a font added to one and not the others is a menu entry that silently renders as the default, which matters when the menu exists so a teacher can pick Atkinson Hyperlegible for a specific child. Shared: labels, families, menu order (`packages/schema/src/fonts.ts`). **Not shared, deliberately:** each surface's fallback STACK (the pipelines differ, which is why the gate asserts font by name) and the renderer's WOFF2 file map (an R2 concern that dies at S9; its `build-fonts.mjs` filename-contract test still runs against the now-private map). The renderer bundle regenerates because typography moved, but its OUTPUT is unchanged — 724 renderer tests pass untouched — so **no `publish-activity` redeploy was needed**.

- **T9/T10 (`eea7c75`) — printed versions, and the questions that must not move.** A version selector driving `printSeed`, and a LABEL on the paper, which is what makes the feature real: printing several arrangements only helps if a teacher can tell the stacks apart afterwards. Lettered ("Version B") because that is what a class period calls it; **version 1 is deliberately unlabelled**, or every worksheet anyone prints would say "Version A". The route also states the easily-missed truth (F12): reprint answer keys after editing, since a content change moves the arrangement under the same seed. **T10's `lockChoiceOrder`** is an optional, additive schema flag (no wire bump, no sanitize change) with an editor toggle, declared on the registry as `shuffleLockedBy` so the transform stays a generic walk rather than growing a per-type switch. No heuristic decides it — "all of the above" is guessable from text, "both A and B" is not.
- **T11 (`c68c443`) — DECISIONS entries** for the answer channel, the print-vs-serve shuffle separation (and the answer-leak defect it closed), and the foldable's clone-the-stylesheets approach with the spike that overturned the ruled one.

**Suite at slice end:** schema 340 / graph-kit 384 / viewer 1018 / renderer 724 / app 1026, plus **61 print e2e** across parity, pagination and answer-key. Typecheck + lint clean; all three bundles regenerate clean.
- **T1 (`3837f62`) — the answer key, keyed by id.** Extraction from the AUTHORED document into a flat block-id-keyed map (D3A), with in-band answers (blanks, math gaps) found by a structural deep walk and out-of-band ones by per-type extractors, each forced into a justified bucket by `ANSWER_KEY_COVERAGE`. **D8A needed no port** — `answerKeyDrawables` and its number-line twin already moved into `@activity/graph-kit/static-svg` with S5's T2. **Found: the renderer's answer key never filled math gaps** (`renderMathBlock` is called without `showAnswers`), so a gap-bearing equation printed a key with nothing in it; the viewer fills them, recorded as a viewer-only improvement the parity gate must NAME rather than assert on both surfaces. The bundle-absence guard asserts code symbols, not prose — worth knowing that leaking the module into `server/index.ts` grew the read bundle only 903 → 987 KiB, **under** the 1500 KiB size ceiling, so the size guard alone would not have caught it.
- **T2 (`843a982`) — the key on paper, derived at render time.** The `AnswerKey` context plus answer branches in every gradable component. Letters and numbers are DERIVED from the order each render draws, never stored, which is what keeps one canonical key correct for every shuffled version. **A decision worth knowing: answer marks are visible on SCREEN**, not gated behind `@media print` like the affordances they sit on — a letter line is a paper affordance, but the letter written on it is content the teacher asked for, and D4A's in-page preview is where they check the key before committing thirty photocopies. `/dev/viewer` gained an `answer key` toggle (`?answers=1`) that T6's gate can drive. Browser-verified, including the negative: with the key off, question twins return to `data-drawables="0"` and only the two DISPLAY variants draw — S5-OV4 intact.
- **T3 (`a3a3ada`) — paper never shows the authored order (D15A).** `print.shuffled` on the PrintSpec (rev-safe by placement, pinned by a `SANITIZER_REV` test) + `applyPrintShuffles`. The generalised guard is the better half: **every serve-shuffled field must ALSO be print-shuffled**, so the next block type cannot repeat the defect. D21C's matching-bank shuffle landed with it. **Found while building: the viewer's `seededShuffle` could deal the IDENTITY** — the renderer has always guaranteed it cannot; see the shuffle deploy below.

- **D24-C ruled + built (`1b7b75f`) — the in-page preview shows the paper.** A question T2 surfaced that the review had not covered: `data-viewer-mode` was on the container root but no CSS read it, so an in-page preview showed content and none of the furniture. Now `[data-viewer-mode='print']` mirrors the printed page's SHOW/HIDE half on screen; geometry (page size, margins, pagination) is deliberately NOT mirrored, because a screen has none of it. CSS cannot share a rule body between a media query and an attribute selector, so this is duplication — held together by a guard test that fails when a selector hidden by print is still visible in the preview.
- **T4 (`ba08777`) — the route, rebuilt in-page.** Three documents now: authored (never rendered), served (real sanitizer → print shuffle), answer key (extracted from authored, own channel). **D20A is a real bug fix:** `savePrint` used to write back the whole document THIS tab loaded, so a teacher with the editor open elsewhere could lose that work by nudging a margin — now it re-fetches and merges only `meta.print`. Plus D23C (upgrade seam before validation), F3 (inert store that rejects loudly), F13 (print rule hiding route chrome AND anything portalled to `document.body`). **Pinned in jsdom, not the browser** — the route needs a signed-in teacher. One mutation that SHOULD have failed didn't, and chasing it was worth it: rendering the authored document changed nothing on the page, because components read answers from the context and never from the block. That is D3A working, and exactly why the assertion moved off the DOM onto the data the container is handed (it now catches twelve answer fields).
- **T5 (`37d64c9`) — the foldable builds from the viewer.** Offscreen render → capture → existing measure/paginate/compose. **The D7A spike changed the answer:** inlining CSS was rejected because KaTeX references its fonts RELATIVELY and they would break in a srcdoc frame (the renderer had papered over this with a CDN rewrite); the app's already-loaded stylesheets carry absolute URLs, so the iframe clones them — correct fonts in dev and prod, no CDN, nothing to keep in sync. Readiness grew a lazy-block check (F4): a captured Suspense fallback becomes a spinner baked into a printed booklet. **Two bugs only the browser could find:** a panel is not a PHONE (the 480px breakpoint fired in a 4-inch panel and flattened an authored 2:1 split to a stack — tracks computed `2fr 1fr` while the row computed `display:block`, so the ratio was right and the layout was gone; now 2.00 exactly), and a panel is not a SCREEN (panels render in screen media, so `@media print` never fired — sheets came out dark on a dark-mode machine with a card border round every panel; documents now pin `data-theme="light"`). Also bounded waits (D11A, replacing an unbounded image wait that could hang "Laying out pages…" forever) and a 300ms rebuild debounce (D14A).
- **T6 (`f756e96`) — the answer key proven equal before the renderer goes (D12A).** Cross-surface, and it compares MEANING not position (F7): each surface's written letter is resolved back through its OWN bank, because the two shuffle differently by design and comparing letters would be permanently red. `math_block` is asserted viewer-only by name, since the renderer never filled math gaps at all. The negative direction is a test too. Runs in CI automatically (the job matches `print-`). **Print e2e reached 60 green across parity / pagination / answer-key at this point** (61 at slice end).

**S5.5 was ENG-REVIEWED + RULED 2026-08-03 (23 decisions, 0 unresolved).** Full rulings + task list (T1–T11): gstack design doc → "S5.5 eng review" section. Headlines: answer channel = real sanitizer client-side + AnswerKey context only the teacher route mounts (D3A); in-page print on the certified S5 path, worksheet iframe deleted (D4A); **base print ALWAYS shuffles ordering + matching via a NEW registry `printShuffled` field OUTSIDE the sanitize spec** — the outside voice caught that the ruled "skip shuffles" default would have printed answer keys (D15A, its strongest finding); randomized print VERSIONS feature added by author (arrangement-only; parameterization → TODOS), resequenced as a post-eviction coda so eviction is never hostage (D16B); foldable re-points via offscreen render → capture → existing measure iframe, fontsource fonts (spike first), bounded waits (D6A/D7A/D11A); cross-surface ANSWER-KEY parity gate must run green BEFORE the eviction commit — impossible after (D12A, semantic assertions never positions); **S5's two pending legs (Linux baselines — regenerate POST-S5.5-DOM per D19B — and contact-sheet read) gate the EVICTION task, not the build** (D9A); eslint no-restricted-imports lands with eviction (D10A); font registry → @activity/schema (D18A); two in-slice bug fixes ruled: savePrint stale-overwrite (D20A) and student matching-dropdown authored-order (D21C). Exit criterion: zero app/viewer imports of @activity/renderer (package survives for publishing until S9). TODOS gained 3 entries (parameterization, batch print, editor upgrade-seam note).

### The never-identity shuffle deploy (2026-08-03)

**✅ RESOLVED + VERIFIED LIVE 2026-08-03 — the never-identity shuffle is deployed on BOTH functions and the pair is back in step.** Verified with `list_edge_functions`, not assumed: **`get-activity` v6 → v7 (`verify_jwt:false`)** and **`check-activity` v3 → v5 (`verify_jwt:true`)**, both ACTIVE, updated **56 seconds apart** — so the window where the grader and the reader disagreed about the served permutation was negligible. All five functions re-checked in the same call and every flag is correct: `ingest-submission` v42 / `get-feedback` v4 / `get-activity` v7 all `verify_jwt:false`; `check-activity` v5 and `publish-activity` v122 both `verify_jwt:true`.

**The 413 that blocked the first attempt is fixed and written up** (`33916c1`, [functions README](../supabase/functions/README.md)): a directory-prefix alias in three `deno.json` files made the CLI upload all ~7 MB of `_shared/` per deploy instead of each function's own imports. Never used by any import; removed. S5.5 contributed ~15 KB — `_shared` was already on the limit.

**Historical note — why these two are ONE change:** The viewer's `seededShuffle` gained the renderer's never-identity guarantee (`packages/viewer/src/sanitize/shuffle.ts`): a fair shuffle deals the authored order back 1/n! of the time, and ordering blocks are allowed as few as TWO items, so one student in two could be served a pre-solved question. Both Edge bundles moved because both read the shuffle — `get-activity` **serves** the permutation, `check-activity` **recomputes** it for S4's ordering omission rule. Deployed apart they disagree about what was served for exactly the seeds that used to land on identity — bounded (an untouched ordering list marked wrong instead of unscored, for that small fraction of student/block pairs) but pointless to accept. **Order does not matter; the gap between them does**, and the gap here was 56 seconds. Nothing else moved on either path: `SANITIZER_REV` is unchanged (pinned by a test), so no read-cache rows were orphaned, and no wire version moved.
