## 2026-08-02 — components-as-data S6 (local-first: buffer, queued checks, tab lock, sign-out, boot paths, service worker) — BUILT V1–V10

Ten-step build of the S6 slice, archived from STATE. Durable reasoning is in
[DECISIONS.md](DECISIONS.md) (six S6 entries); the build-order plan and rulings are in the
gstack design doc's "S6 eng review" section. Headline: everything that protects student
work is built and browser-verified; one promise (offline reopen against the built worker)
is parked with evidence in TODOS.md.

**Bugs the build found in its own earlier steps — the pattern worth remembering:** V6
found that V1's orphan GC silently deleted unsent work on republish; V7 found that V3's
tab lock never told the losing tab it lost (jsdom has no Web Locks, so no unit test could
have) and that V4's hardened `signOutEverything` had zero callers; V9 found that the
worker installs during the first visit so nothing is cached until the third. Each was
invisible to the level of testing that had signed the previous step off.

**▶ V1 LANDED 2026-08-02 (viewer package only; nothing to deploy).** The persisted shape now carries `userId` + `pending` + `inFlight` and refuses a foreign-user blob on the way in (`hydrateViewerState(raw, expectedUserId)`); `createViewerStore` REQUIRES a `userId`. New `store/buffer.ts` (key `activity-viewer:buffer:<user>:<activity>:<version>` — under the prefix `signOutEverything` already purges, so sign-out finds it; ~300 ms debounce + flush-on-hide; quota/unavailable degradation that keeps work pending rather than dropping it; `sweepForeignBuffers` boot sweep + `sweepOrphanVersions` GC; a `canWrite` gate so V3's lock can stop a stale tab WRITING, not just typing) and `store/ports.ts` (`Clock` + `HideSignal`; V2's `ConnectivitySignal` joins them). **S6-3's overturn is live:** the in-flight idempotency key moved out of the module-local map into state and therefore into the buffer, so a lost response + closed lid retries as a REPLAY instead of minting a second attempt — the old "deliberately NOT persisted" comment is replaced with the reasoning that superseded it. `studentAuth.ts` now IMPORTS `VIEWER_STORAGE_PREFIX` from the viewer instead of restating it (the two literals could drift; one of them is the purge contract). 28 new tests in `packages/viewer/tests/buffer.test.ts`; suite 890 viewer / 993 app / 3331 workspace green, typecheck + lint clean, no bundle drift. **Not yet wired into the route on purpose:** the buffer is constructed nowhere — V2 mounts it alongside the queue executor so the hydrate/save/dispose lifecycle is written once rather than twice.

  **▶ V2 LANDED 2026-08-02 (viewer + the route mount; still nothing to deploy).** Offline is now a DELAY, not a failure: a check that can't reach the server becomes `pending` (the derived phase — `pending` map is the only source, rebuilt on hydrate so it survives a reload) and fires itself on reconnect. `store/queue.ts` owns no queue — it reads the store's `pending`, fires sequentially (a classroom reconnecting is 30 devices; one in flight per student keeps us under the check RPC's per-student ceiling), refreshes the session BEFORE firing (a long outage means an expired token, and firing first would burn the attempt on a 401 that has nothing to do with the student's work) and PARKS on a failed refresh with the intent intact. Drift vs the enqueue-time fingerprint drives "Checked your latest answers" (2.2A). `ConnectivitySignal` joined the ports; `navigator.onLine` is used as a TRIGGER only — the real test of connectivity is sending the check. **A real bug the suite caught:** the first executor dropped a wake signal that arrived while a run was in flight, so a run started moments before the network returned would swallow the very edge it was waiting for; fixed with a coalescing re-run (never two runs at once, never a missed edge). **Route mount landed too** — `StudentViewer` now runs the boot sweeps → hydrate → subscribe(save) → `queue.start()` in one effect scoped to (user, version), so V1's buffer is live rather than dead code. 24 queue tests + 4 route-mount tests; **both new gates mutation-verified red** (deleting the sweep and reverting the coalescing fix each turn the suite red). Suite 914 viewer / 997 app / 3359 workspace green; typecheck + lint clean; no bundle drift; e2e unchanged (202 pass, same one pre-existing red below). **Still V6's, not done here:** offline-tolerant auth boot, inline re-auth UI, and the republish boot path.

  **▶ V3 LANDED 2026-08-02 (viewer + route; still nothing to deploy).** One editable tab per (student, activity), via **`navigator.locks`** rather than the heartbeat 2.3A sketched — the lock releases automatically when a tab dies, which deletes the staleness guess that Chromebook tab-throttling would otherwise make wrong in both directions. `store/tabLock.ts` holds an exclusive lock for the tab's life; a second tab is read-only and queued; "Use it here" is `steal: true`, and **the steal's rejection IS the notification** — the displaced tab learns from the lock itself, so the ruled BroadcastChannel was deliberately NOT built (a second mechanism carrying the same fact could disagree with it; noted here so the deviation is visible, not silent). A demoted tab re-queues, so closing the thief hands the activity back with no reload. No Web Locks support ⇒ assume single tab: locking a student out of their own work is worse than the clobber risk. **Read-only is one `<fieldset disabled>` around the worksheet**, not a prop threaded through 22 block components — the browser disables every control inside, including ones a future block type adds, so a block cannot forget to honor it; canvas surfaces aren't form controls, so `[data-graph-canvas]` gets a `pointer-events: none` companion rule (without it a stale tab could drag a point, update the store, and have the write silently refused). The buffer's `canWrite` gate from V1 is now wired to the lock (outside-voice #7). 10 lock tests (against a real lock *broker* fake that queues, grants, and implements steal per spec) + 6 container tests; **both new gates mutation-verified red.** Suite 949 viewer / 997 app / 3394 workspace green; e2e 202 pass (print gate unaffected by the fieldset — the CSS reset holds). **The two-tab flow itself is a V7 e2e row by plan** — jsdom has no `navigator.locks`, so the route falls back to single-tab there and only real chromium can exercise the handoff.

  **▶ V4 LANDED 2026-08-02 (app auth + the viewer cache contract; nothing to deploy).** Closes the verified hole the outside voice found: `@supabase/auth-js` `signOut()` **returns early on a network failure without clearing the local session**, so a student tapping Sign out on dying school Wi-Fi walked away leaving the next student signed in as them — every downstream per-user guard correct and irrelevant, because the session itself named the wrong person. `signOutEverything` now falls back to `signOut({ scope: 'local' })` when the network call fails (and still throws, so the caller can say "couldn't reach the server" — but the device is already clean by then). **New `watchIdleSignOut`** adds S6-6's escalation: prompt after the quiet stretch, and if nothing answers within a 2-minute grace, sign out locally rather than leaving a live session on a shared cart between periods; activity mid-grace dismisses it. `onUserActivity` was extracted so the watcher and the grace share ONE definition of "a human is doing something" — two definitions would drift, and the drifted one decides whether to sign a student out. **New `packages/viewer/src/store/caches.ts` pins the SW cache naming contract BEFORE V8 writes the worker** (same direction as the buffer prefix): `activity-viewer:cache:shell` for app code vs `activity-viewer:cache:content:<userId>` for per-student documents, with `sweepForeignCaches` (boot) and `purgeStudentCaches` (sign-out). The user scoping is load-bearing, not tidiness — the Cache API keys by URL and ignores auth, while this platform serves a per-student SHUFFLED document from one URL, so a shared content cache would hand student B the copy built for A and grade B against the wrong shuffle. **Deliberate refinement of R5, flagged rather than silent: the shell is NOT purged at sign-out.** R5 asked for the caches holding student WORK; the shell holds application code identical for every user and no student data, so wiping it would make the next student re-download the app over school Wi-Fi and defeat the offline capability precisely on the shared devices that need it most. 9 cache tests + 8 auth tests; **the local-fallback test is the one that would have caught the original bug.**

  **▶ V5 LANDED 2026-08-02 (e2e infra; nothing to deploy).** The signed-in harness that GATES V7's failure matrix. Every e2e until now drove `/playground` (editor, no auth, no network), and the S6 cases *are* "a signed-in student on a dying network" — half of them mean nothing without an identity. Google SSO can't run in CI, so the harness fakes exactly two things and **changes no shipped code**: (1) a SESSION written into the storage key supabase-js reads at startup, so the real client behaves as it does for a real student — no test-only branch anywhere in the app; (2) the SERVER, via Playwright route interception matched **by path, not origin**, so the stub doesn't care what `VITE_SUPABASE_URL` is. Offline is `route.abort('internetdisconnected')` — a real dead network makes fetch REJECT, and only that path produces the viewer's `offline` kind; a 5xx would take a different branch and prove nothing. **New `student` Playwright project on its own dev server (port+1, `reuseExistingServer: false`) with PINNED Supabase env** — the session key is derived from the configured URL, so a reused server carrying the author's real `.env.local` would derive a different key and silently land every student spec on the sign-in screen; Vite gives process env precedence over `.env` files, so the pin wins. The editor lane is untouched (still reuses, still 203). **The one supabase-js internal this leans on (the `sb-<ref>-auth-token` derivation) is pinned by a unit test** that writes a session under the derived key and asserts a REAL client reads it back — because a changed scheme would otherwise surface as "every student spec sees the sign-in screen", a symptom with a dozen plausible causes and the harness the last place anyone would look. 5 proof specs, all green in real chromium, including **offline → pending → reconnect → checked**: the first time V1–V3's local-first work has been exercised in a browser rather than jsdom. Mutation-verified (dropping `signInAs` turns it red). **e2e now 208 pass** (203 editor + 5 student), unit 1007 app.

  **▶ V6 LANDED 2026-08-02 (route boot paths + viewer document cache; nothing to deploy).** **It opened by finding a silent data-loss bug V1 shipped:** `sweepOrphanVersions` ran unconditionally on mount, so a teacher republishing overnight DELETED the buffer of every student who had left work or a queued check unsent — no error, no trace, the student's only symptom being that their work was gone. That is precisely what S6-9 exists to prevent, and the GC now has exactly one exception (`bufferHasUnsentWork`), tested from both sides so it still collects spent buffers and the quota ceiling stays gone. Four behaviors landed: (1) **offline boot** — a failed load with kind `offline` falls back to the on-device document instead of an error screen a student already knows the content of; (2) **the republish path, both branches** — unsent work on a superseded version renders THAT version pinned when the device still holds its document (the grader accepts non-current versions by design, so the queued check can still fire), and otherwise preserves the blob behind an honest "an earlier check couldn't run; your answers are saved"; (3) **inline re-auth (2.3A)** — the queue's `onAuthRequired` (wired but unused since V2) now raises a passive banner with a sign-in action, cleared automatically when `ensureSession` recovers, never a modal, work saving locally throughout; (4) **banner dedup** (outside-voice 13) — at most ONE banner shows, ordered most-actionable first (expired session → stranded work → offline copy → pinned version), because three stacked sentences is a wall a student skips. New `viewer/src/store/documentCache.ts` keeps the served document per (user, activity, version) in localStorage — deliberately NOT in the buffer blob, which is rewritten on a debounce as the student types, and a 40 KB document re-serialized every few keystrokes is how a Chromebook starts feeling broken. The key grammar was generalized (`activity-viewer:<kind>:<user>:<activity>:<version>`, kind ∈ buffer|doc) so ONE sweep covers both stores; `sweepForeignBuffers` → **`sweepForeignStorage`**, now also removing anything under the prefix it cannot attribute (sign-out already deletes the whole prefix, so sparing those was the one inconsistency between the two paths that clean a shared device). 12 document-cache tests + 5 boot-path tests; **both new behaviors mutation-verified red.** Suite 970 viewer / 1012 app / 3430 workspace; e2e 208. **Known limitation, honest:** offline boot needs a still-valid token — if the access token has expired AND the network is down, supabase-js cannot refresh and the route shows the pre-auth gate. Handling that would mean reading supabase's storage directly in shipped code, which is the internal V5 deliberately confined to the test harness.

  **▶ V7 LANDED 2026-08-02 (the failure matrix in a real browser; nothing to deploy).** 14 e2e rows re-proving every S6 promise end to end — work survives an outage, queued checks fire on reconnect and on boot after a closed lid, the drift notice tells the truth, a reconnect storm still records ONE attempt, a server error is a failure while offline is a delay, a dead session is a banner not a wall, a second tab cannot clobber the first, a shared device leaks nothing, a republish keeps the student on their version, offline reopen shows the saved copy. Every absence-shaped row SEEDS what it expects gone first. **It found two real defects that jsdom structurally could not see.** (1) **The losing tab was never told it lost.** `createTabLock` only fired `onChange` on a TRANSITION, and a plain queued Web Locks request is silent when the lock is taken — it just never resolves — so "will never be granted" was indistinguishable from "not granted yet"; combined with the route's optimistic default, the second tab stayed editable and could clobber the first. Fixed with an `ifAvailable` probe (the only way to learn "someone else holds this" without waiting for a turn that may never come) plus a first-determination notification; regression-pinned at unit level now that the broker models `ifAvailable`. jsdom has no `navigator.locks`, so **every** jsdom test had been taking the single-tab branch. (2) **`signOutEverything` had ZERO callers** — V4 hardened a function nothing invoked, and `Home.tsx` (the app's only sign-out control) called raw `supabase.auth.signOut()`, so the purge never ran and the offline-signOut hole stayed open for teachers. Home now calls it. Also closed a V6 gap the matrix exposed: a **401 mid-check** is session expiry too, and now converges on the same passive re-auth banner as the queue's path rather than surfacing as a lone section error. **One test design note worth keeping:** the two-tab row asserts the guarantee (*exactly one* tab editable, and takeover decides which) rather than which tab wins — StrictMode remounts the effect in dev, releasing and re-requesting the lock, so the initial winner is genuinely either tab; asserting an ordering the lock never promised would have been a permanently flaky test. **⚠ CORRECTED at V8:** the "stable over 4 runs" claim below was measured at LANE level and did not hold per-spec — the clobber row failed 4 runs in 5 in isolation, because its own settling poll re-made the very assumption the row above had just abandoned (it required `page` specifically to be the live tab). Fixed by following the race instead of assuming it: settle, identify the live tab, then test the guarantee. Now stable 5/5 in isolation and 3/3 at lane level. **e2e 222 pass** (203 editor + 19 student), unit 3432.

  **▶ V8 LANDED 2026-08-02 (service worker; nothing to deploy).** `vite-plugin-pwa` generateSW + autoUpdate, registered explicitly in `main.tsx`, **disabled in dev** (a dev worker diverges from the generated one, so specs against it would prove nothing — V9 owns the preview-build lane). **Two deliberate departures from the ruled config, both flagged rather than slipped in.** (1) **Precache is the navigation document ALONE — `precache 1 entries (0.96 KiB)`.** The ruled "shell-only globPatterns" reads well but this app's entry chunk is **~3 MB** (editor and viewer share one bundle today), and a glob wide enough to catch it also catches sibling chunks no single route needs. Every asset filename is content-hashed and therefore immutable, so runtime CacheFirst gives the same offline result while downloading exactly what the student actually opened — which is what S6-11 was protecting. (2) **The API is not cached by the worker at all**, which retires the user-scoped-runtime-cache design from S6-5/T2 rather than implementing it: the Cache API keys by URL and ignores auth, and the document a student needs offline is already kept per-user by V6's `documentCache`, where the sign-out purge and boot sweep reach it. V4's `caches.ts` contract stays as the guard rail for any future per-user cache (its `purgeStudentCaches` call in sign-out is now defence-in-depth with no producer — a judgment call worth revisiting if it never gains one). **Lazy-chunk 404 recovery** (a today-bug the SW discussion surfaced, not a SW bug): Pages replaces hashed assets on deploy, so a tab open across a deploy hits a 404 the first time a student opens the calculator or a graph — a control that silently does nothing. `vite:preloadError` → one guarded reload, the guard in sessionStorage so a broken deploy cannot loop, cleared on the next healthy load. **Production-gated**, after an ungated version turned a transient dev-server import hiccup into a mid-test reload ("execution context destroyed") — the failure it fixes is a deploy artifact and neither content hashing nor deploys exist in dev. 5 recovery tests, loop guard mutation-verified. Unit 1017 app; e2e 222 stable across repeated runs.

  **▶ V9 LANDED 2026-08-02 (preview-build lane; nothing to deploy) — with ONE PROMISE STILL UNPROVEN, parked visibly.** New `sw` Playwright project on its own `vite preview` server (port+2, pinned env, never reused), because the worker only exists in a build — `workers: 1`, non-parallel, since a worker outlives a page and one spec's registration would control the next spec's first navigation. **5 rows green against the real worker:** it installs and CLAIMS the page (skipWaiting + clientsClaim — the "stuck on last week's build" trap); the precache holds **exactly one entry** (`index.html`), pinning S6-11 against a future glob that would drag in the 3 MB entry; hashed assets land in the runtime cache; **no `/functions/v1/` URL is ever cached** (the per-student-shuffle hazard, now a browser-level guarantee, not a config intention); and sign-out clears student data while KEEPING the shell (V4's deliberate exception, verified end to end). **A real defect found and fixed:** the worker installs DURING the first visit, so that visit's own asset requests never pass through it and nothing is cached — offline would not have worked until a student's THIRD visit. `warmAssetCache` now seeds the runtime cache from `performance.getEntriesByType('resource')`, i.e. exactly what the page actually used, re-fetched almost free because the assets are immutable and already in the HTTP cache. **⚠ UNPROVEN: the two `offline reopen` rows are `test.fixme`, not deleted.** Online, everything checks out (`workerStart` non-zero for both the document and the entry chunk, so the CacheFirst route matches; assets confirmed in Cache Storage before going offline). Under `context.setOffline(true)` the navigation is served 200 and the page stays controlled, but parse-time subresources die `net::ERR_FAILED` while a `fetch()` for the SAME url from page script resolves 200 seconds later. Route-abort behaves identically and so does a run with NO interception, which rules out Playwright's routing. Evidence points at the emulated-offline state rather than at this config — but pointing is not proving, and a green test built on that guess would be worse than the note. TODOS.md carries it with the full reasoning and the next thing to try (stop the preview server instead of emulating); **it should close before S9 cutover**, which is when students meet the path. e2e **227 pass, 2 parked** (203 editor + 19 student + 5 sw).

  **✅ RESOLVED 2026-08-02 (`988e701`) — the `dark-contrast.e2e.ts:97` red was a TOKEN NAMESPACE COLLISION, not a bad hex.** White on dark-mode `accent-strong` measured 1.80 because `@activity/viewer/tokens.css` — imported by StudentViewer/DevViewer, both eagerly reached from `App.tsx`, so it loads on EVERY route — declared onto the shared `:root` **unlayered**, while the app's Tailwind `@theme` compiles into `@layer theme`. Unlayered beats layered whatever the source order, so the viewer silently won **all seven** names the two layers shared. `accent-strong` was the sharp one because they mean OPPOSITE things by it: in the viewer it is accent's HOVER partner (dark BRIGHTENS it to `#93c5fd`, dark ink on top), in the app it backs a solid WHITE-text button (dark must stay dark). Neither hex was wrong — the shared NAME was. Fix: the viewer's `--color-*`/`--radius-*`/`--font-*`/`--shadow-*` became `--vw-*`, a pure rename (viewer.css is byte-identical after un-prefixing, so viewer pixels are untouched); the app chrome silently regained its ratified fix-6 `ink`/`line`/`accent`, and Tailwind's `--radius-md/lg` stopped being retuned to 4px/6px under every `rounded-md`/`rounded-lg`. **Standing rule, now guard-enforced** (`packages/viewer/tests/tokens.test.ts`): no viewer token may sit in a namespace Tailwind's `@theme` owns — alias explicitly (`--vw-color-x: var(--color-x)`) if a value really should be shared. Removing the leak also exposed two latent editor bugs it had been masking, fixed in the same commit by splitting `--ed-accent-strong` (one token was serving both "fill under white text" and "accent text on a dark tint", which pull opposite ways in dark): white-on-command-bar-primary-hover was 1.80 and its base 2.42. e2e now **203 pass**.

  **✅ ALSO FIXED 2026-08-02 (`f0df8b5`) — the S6 local-first tests were racing React's effect flush.** `StudentViewer.test.tsx`'s "persists the student's work" / "restores that work" failed ~1 run in 4 under full `pnpm test` load and never in isolation — pre-existing since V1–V3, confirmed on clean main at `e8e212e`. Diagnosis came from the signature `expected [] to have a length of 1`: nothing written at all, and `buffer.write()` bails on `if (!dirty) return`, with `dirty` set only by `save()`, which is wired by `store.subscribe(() => buffer.save())` **inside the route's effect**. `findAllByRole` resolves on the COMMIT, not the passive effect, and the served-activity fetch resolves outside `act` — so the test could type into a store nobody was subscribed to. Fixed in the shared `firstBlank()` helper (flush effects so "ready" means mounted AND wired); nothing asserted was weakened. Verified with **30 consecutive full-monorepo runs** green. Worth remembering as a class: any test that types before the mount effect has run is testing a store with no listeners.
- **S7 analytics census**, **S8 perf-budget CI**, **S9 cutover** — later; S9 needs both parity gates green.

---

## 2026-08-01 — components-as-data S4 (server grading, live) + S5 (student print + the gate) (archived from STATE 2026-08-04)

Both slices reviewed, built, and verified in one day. Rulings: gstack design doc → "S4 eng review" (17 decisions) + "S5 eng review" (17 decisions). Durable reasoning: DECISIONS.md → "Check-RPC latency" and the S5 parity-artifact entries.

### S5 — student print mode + the print gate

**S5 ENG-REVIEWED + RULED (17 decisions, 0 unresolved); BUILD T1–T4, T6–T10, T12 DONE; the PARITY GATE RAN GREEN IN CI (51 print e2e at slice end)** (viewer 714 → 845). The two author legs (Linux baselines, contact-sheet read) closed in S5.5 — see the 2026-08-03 section above.

**S5 T1a DONE (`06681df`) — the viewer's print layer stopped being a sketch.** Per-type page breaks (the stylesheet had been applying `break-inside: avoid` to `.viewer-block` wholesale, silently contradicting the registry for the seven `auto` types — paragraphs and lists were being held whole across page breaks); a unit guard now parses the break list back out of the CSS and fails on drift from the registry (CSS can't import TS, so this is the only thing holding them together). **Paper affordances** — circle-me letter per choice, write-the-letter line per matching item, number-the-steps box per ordering row — rendered ALWAYS and revealed by `@media print`, never built on demand, because the browser's own File→Print gives no hook to prepare in; the tests deliberately render in SCREEN mode since that's the mode students actually print from. Letters come from one shared helper matching the renderer's convention (Matching's private alphabet constant is gone). Plus the full treatment vocabulary (blanks→writing lines, MC letters with controls hidden, matching bank + lines, ordering boxes, writing boxes with the essay taller, figure caps, scaffold boxes with the faded example dashed, callout border styles, live board hidden). **S5-9 correction worth knowing: the dark→light print flattening ALREADY EXISTED in tokens.css** — the review's claim that it was missing was wrong (`--color-ink` really is `#f1f5f9` in dark mode, but the print block already forces `#000000`). What was actually owed was the GUARD, now added + red-green tested, so the next print rule reaching for an unflattened colour token fails a test instead of failing on paper.

**S5 T1b DONE (`1f650d6`) — authored layout finally reaches the page.** The viewer had been dropping `Column.width` weights, `Column.minHeight` work space, and per-block `width`/`align` **entirely** — a teacher's 2:1 split rendered 50/50 and a half-width figure rendered full width, on screen and on paper. Rows are now a grid (flex's `1 1 0` cannot express weights by construction); the track list and footprint arrive as inline `--activity-*` custom properties mirroring the renderer's `--columns-template`/`--block-width`/`data-block-align`, which is also what lets the phone breakpoint collapse them (an inline width would follow a student to 375px). Browser-verified with a synthetic 2:1 row: ratio exactly 2.00, 8rem floor → 128px, half-width block 225px inside a 449px column, left-align zeroing the margin. **This is why S5-OV2 exists** — none of these is a block, so the per-block roster could never have caught them.

**⚠ SIBLING FIX APPLIED (author, 2026-08-01):** `short_answer` and `essay` joined `self_explanation` in `breakInside: 'avoid'`, extending S5-OV6 to the two unnamed members of the writing-box family. A rule-based guard now asserts the WHOLE family avoids, so a future free-text type can't ship with a prompt strandable from its answer space.

**S5 T1c DONE (`bf2fc44`) — the document print layer.** `@page` size/margin (a real per-document `<style>` rule, NOT a custom property — `@page` can't read them, and a `var()` attempt would silently fall back to letter on every A4 printer), the `--print-*` vars on the worksheet root (problem spacing + work space scoped to `data-block-category="question"` so prose keeps its own rhythm), the print header (renders NOTHING when all fields are off — an empty ruled box reads as a load failure; `aria-hidden`, since blank lines for a pen have nothing to announce), and the reference print box honoring `printReferencePanel` in both directions. Browser-verified: `@page{size:letter;margin:0.5in;}` injected exactly once, vars on the root, header in the DOM and `display:none` on screen.

**✅ GLOSSARY APPENDIX DONE (`650b823`) — T1 COMPLETE.** The paper surface for inline definitions: a structural (not type-directed) collection walk ported from the renderer + a 7-variant `DefinitionBlock` renderer, gated on `printDefinitionGlossary`, appended after every section. **The integration that decides it works:** a test runs a real authored doc through the REAL sanitizer and asserts the appendix still finds the definition — the viewer only ever sees sanitized documents, and the sanitizer walks inline nodes hunting secrets, which is structurally what a definition mark looks like; had it stripped them, every glossary would print empty while all the other tests passed. **Fixture gap closed:** NO fixture carried a definition (they're an inline MARK — no per-block fixture can hold one), so the gate's `document/definition-glossary` case would have passed vacuously. The paragraph fixture now carries a real defined term ("slope"). Ported not imported, deliberately: the viewer must not depend on the renderer it replaces (that import dangles at S5.5); two copies until then, held equal by the gate's document fixture — the S4-6 posture.

**S5 T2 DONE (`eb30d5f`) — kit-free static SVG print twins.** Every kit-backed block renders two figures: the live JSXGraph board for screen, and a static twin that prints. **The extraction:** `graph-svg`/`number-line-svg`/`data-plot-svg` moved wholesale from the renderer into **`@activity/graph-kit/static-svg`** — a pure subpath (no DOM/JSXGraph/MathLive, the `/scorers` discipline). graph-kit is the package both surfaces already depend on and the one that outlives cutover, so it's the only home adding no dependency edge and leaving nothing dangling at S5.5; the renderer re-exports, and **its 724 tests passing unchanged is the proof the move was behaviour-preserving**. `escape`/`attr` moved with them. **The OV4 rule is live and TYPE-ENFORCED:** questions print empty axes, display figures print authored drawables — narrowed on the interaction, so only the display variant *has* a `drawables` field and a question's twin cannot carry one. Both surfaces emit `data-drawables` from that one renderer, which is what makes the gate's count assertion mean anything. **Browser-verified across all 13 kit instances:** 11 question variants at `0`, display graph `1`, display chart `5`, all hidden on screen; visually, clean labelled graph paper vs the same grid plus its authored point. **Bug caught in passing:** `export … from` creates no local binding and `html.ts`'s own helpers call `attr()` — compiled fine, failed at runtime on 107 renderer tests. **Bundles regenerated + leak-checked:** viewer-server 903.1 KiB, grading 2519.3 KiB, renderer 41.8/49.1 KiB — zero JSXGraph/MathLive code in any.

**S5 T3 DONE (`7ccf2e5`) — the print action + readiness barrier.** The Print control waits for pending math to actually render, `document.fonts` to settle, and every image to load or fail — all bounded by one shared budget, printing anyway on expiry with a report naming what didn't settle (a student who *can't* print is worse off than one missing a figure). **It waits on the DOM, not promises:** an earlier draft awaited `loadMathRenderer()`, which is a lie by one tick — the engine being resident ≠ React having re-rendered with it; it polls for `[data-math-pending]` to clear. **Two real implementation bugs the tests forced out:** (1) racing fonts/images against `sleep(remaining)` meant the LOSING arm still consumed the whole budget, so whichever check ran last got none of it — all three now poll one predicate against a shared deadline; (2) load/error listeners miss events that already fired — polling `img.complete` is the whole check, and it goes true on failure too, so a 404 settles instead of holding the budget. The image test double had the same disease (reported `complete:false` forever) and now models real browser behaviour. **Ctrl+P still races the chunk** — accepted residual (`beforeprint` is synchronous, no hook to hold), survivable because every fallback is built legible; S8 trigger already in TODOS. Placement is the top bar per S5-3; also mounted in `/dev/viewer` so T7's gate has something to drive. **Browser-verified live:** with pending math injected, the control reads "Preparing…" + disabled, reports `{ready:true, waited:["math"], timedOut:[]}`, and calls `print()` exactly once.

**S5 T4 DONE (`6afa398`) — the teacher's chosen worksheet font reaches students.** The viewer had ignored `meta.typography` entirely (the `--activity-font-family` seam existed since S0 and nothing set it). Not cosmetic: the fonts on offer are Atkinson Hyperlegible/Andika (low vision, early readers), Lexend (fluency), Comic Neue (dyslexia) — dropping the setting removes an accessibility decision made for a specific child. **One loader, two consumers:** the app's editor loader moved INTO the viewer (the app already depends on it), so there aren't two switch statements over five font ids to drift. `default` returns null rather than a stack, so the stylesheet's own token stays in charge. The root also carries `data-activity-font` — an id is comparable across surfaces whose font PIPELINES differ by design, where a computed family string isn't (S5-4's "assert the name, never the pixels"). **Browser-verified:** selecting Atkinson sets the attribute + var, resolves in computed style, AND registers the family in `document.fonts` — the dynamic `@fontsource` import really fetched files.

**S5 T6 DONE (`1acfb04`) — `printExpectations(type, ctx)` is the gate's contract.** The single location for the per-block print rules asserted on BOTH surfaces, plus the three fixture rosters (blocks derived from the registry; structural for columns/sizing/confidence; document for header/reference/glossary/page-size/print-vars/typography — the classes a per-block roster is structurally blind to). Named honestly per S5-5: only break-inside/break-after and the roster derive from PrintSpec; the rest is a written per-block table with a per-surface selector map, because a graph grid caps at 3.5in while a number line caps at 5in under the same treatment. **Ruling S5-OV6 landed with it** — `math_block`, `data_plot`, `self_explanation` now declare `break-inside: avoid`; the registry comment that said "T8 decides" now records the decision. **Verified, not assumed:** `SANITIZER_REV` still `1-f8328527` (the rev hashes sanitize specs only, so the print change orphaned no cache rows and needed **no `get-activity` redeploy**); `printExpectations` absent from the regenerated viewer-server bundle (it imports FROM the registry and is never re-exported by `server/index.ts` — the V9 leak cannot repeat); all six guards **red-green tested by mutation** (dropping the solutions rule, duplicating a callout border style, printing empty axes for a DISPLAY figure — the OV4 bug, dropping a document roster entry, and skipping a check on one or both surfaces each turn the suite red). Viewer 714 → 742.

**✅ S5 T7 DONE (`b6c1809`) — THE PARITY GATE RAN, 35/35 GREEN.** Every registered type on BOTH surfaces, the variants that change a printed rule, a no-network proof, a dark-mode print case (closing S5-9), and a print-after-check case. **The first runs found SEVEN real defects** — in the table, the gate, and the fixtures — before it guarded anything: `drawable-count` read the container not the SVG; S5-OV6 improvements were being asserted on the renderer that deliberately still has the old behaviour (now viewer-only BY NAME with reasons — this is what makes the gate spec-referenced rather than output-referenced); `bare-underline` measured border STYLE when Tailwind's preflight sets `solid` on everything with width 0; the lazy-component wait was satisfied by other blocks' wrappers so graph twins were measured before mounting; **callout had ONE fixture** when the rule that matters is that four variants stay distinguishable in grayscale; variant checks ran against whichever instance was first; and two more viewer-only improvements surfaced (the renderer gives every free-text block the same box, and leaves a static `graph_figure` uncapped). **⚠ AND a vacuous-pass hole the red-green pass exposed:** deleting the "solutions never print" rule did NOT turn the gate red, because pills/feedback/solutions only exist AFTER a check — the rules were asserted about elements never on the page. Fixed by checking a section first (the harness now scripts solutions); the same mutation now fails. Also: a suppressed check is now OMITTED rather than emitted as unrunnable — the guard suite rejected the latter, correctly.

**✅ ALL THREE FIXTURE CLASSES GATED (`b564269`) — 48 cases.** The block roster was gated; structural and document were declared but unasserted. Twelve new cases via harness query params: a 2:1 column row prints side by side **in its authored ratio** (a split that prints 50/50 has lost the teacher's layout as surely as one that stacks), footprint sizing survives, reserved work space is a real floor, sections flow; header fields print as labelled rules in order, A4 reaches `@page`, configured spacing lands on QUESTIONS not prose, and the reference box / glossary each print when on **and are ABSENT when off** (the second direction is what catches a component ignoring the setting — how a teacher ends up with a reference sheet on every worksheet in a unit). Red-green proven both ways.

**✅ S5 T8 DONE (`3fd891c`) — the gate RUNS IN CI, plus the baseline path.** Its own job (needs a browser + dev server; a browser failure should read as "print parity broke", not as a mystery inside `test`), Chromium-only, failure traces uploaded. **Deliberately scoped to print-parity** — the editor's 145-case e2e suite stays local-only; folding it in would cost runtime and make this job's failures ambiguous. **Screenshot baselines** land as the layer that catches what the rules don't NAME (a collapsed margin breaks no rule and still ruins a worksheet); viewer-against-viewer only, since comparing the two surfaces is exactly what S5-6 rejected. Baselines are Linux/CI-authoritative (S5-7); generated + committed in S5.5 (see the 2026-08-03 section). CI never rewrites its own expectations.

**✅ S5 T9 DONE (`dbfc151`) — pagination, the layer print emulation cannot reach.** `emulateMedia({media:'print'})` applies print CSS in ONE CONTINUOUS VIEWPORT and never paginates, so every break rule checked there was a computed VALUE, not a fact about layout. `page.pdf()` is the only thing running Chromium's paged-media layout. **The key test caught itself being useless first:** "the break rules change the layout" compared the fixture worksheet with rules on vs off and got **9 pages either way** (its blocks are small enough that none would straddle, so nothing has to be pushed) — written with `toBeGreaterThanOrEqual`, it passed on that equality and proved nothing. It now makes blocks taller than half a page so `avoid` MUST push each one whole: **32 vs 24 pages**, strict inequality. **Deliberately NOT covered:** per-block "this problem didn't straddle" — Chromium's PDF text is in compressed streams, needing a parser this repo doesn't carry, and the contact sheet shows a human the same property. A decision, recorded.

**✅ S5 T10 DONE (`d7c4832`) — the contact sheet.** A generated side-by-side (every block, renderer beside viewer, both in print media) for the half of parity that isn't automatable. Outside the gate and outside CI on purpose: it produces something to LOOK at, and a "test" whose result is a person's opinion shouldn't go red on a push. Generated + checked (22 rows, 44 images, all references resolving); output gitignored. Signed off 2026-08-03 (see above).

**✅ S5 T12 DONE (`8ac8cd9`) — DECISIONS entries** for the parity artifact (and why cross-surface pixel diff was rejected), the spec-referenced property that makes an improvement expressible, the three fixture classes, the pagination gap, and the static-SVG extraction. **T11 satisfied by construction** — the fixtures module already pins `FIXTURE_SHUFFLE_SEED`, so ordering fixtures are deterministic and print baselines can't flake on a reshuffle.

**S5 REVIEW SUMMARY.** Scope re-cut with the author: **S5 = student print mode + parity gate; S5.5 = committed teacher-print migration** (ActivityPrint on the viewer tree, answer-key variant, foldable re-point), gated on S5 green; the renderer fully deletes only after S5.5 ("retirement" at S9 = student path). **R4 CLOSED — the parity artifact is defined and it is NOT cross-surface pixel diff** (rejected: two different DOM/font systems make any threshold permanently red or vacuously loose). The gate = PrintSpec-derived `printExpectations` DOM/computed-style assertions on BOTH surfaces + viewer-only screenshot baselines (linux-CI authoritative) + a `page.pdf()` pagination pass + one recorded contact-sheet sign-off. The outside voice (11 findings) **amended 4 in-review rulings**: document-level print features (header/reference box/glossary/@page/meta.print vars) are STUDENT surface and stay in S5 with document fixtures; display-variant graphs print their AUTHORED drawables via graph-svg **extracted** from the renderer (question variants print empty axes); the print-readiness barrier awaits fonts + images, not just lazy chunks; the harness serves KaTeX assets locally so the network block degrades neither surface. Also ruled: structural fixtures (columns/sizing/confidence rows — the per-block roster is layout-blind); the two "faithful print oddities" **FIXED in PrintSpec now** (math_block + data_plot breakInside, self_explanation prompt/box cohesion); dark-mode print **forces light tokens** (found in-review: `var(--color-ink)` underlines could print white-on-white; pin + dark-mode e2e fixture); meta.typography wired in S5 (@fontsource, never R2); top-bar Print action now, 7.3A's completion-panel/chip placements land with their chrome. Full record: gstack design doc → "S5 eng review" (rulings S5-0…S5-abs, 12 tasks T1–T12, failure modes, rosters); test plan artifact `user-main-eng-review-test-plan-20260801-134500.md`; TODOS.md gained the S8-keyed eager-KaTeX trigger.

### S4 — server-authoritative grading, built and live

**S4 REVIEWED 2026-08-01 (`/plan-eng-review`, FULL).** 17 decisions ruled; outside voice raised 13 findings, **3 of which AMENDED rulings made earlier in the same review**: the rate limiter (the read path's isolate limiter is documented in-repo as nearly inert → DB-backed per-student instead), the grades widening (freezing `unique(check_id, block_id)` before the teacher UI that decides attempts-vs-latest → **cut from S4**), and the previously-unruled empty-check solution reveal (→ parity kept, property recorded). The **authorization chain was the plan's one security hole** and is now a required branch pin. Full rulings + build order: the "S4 eng review" section of the gstack design doc. Test plan artifact: `user-main-eng-review-test-plan-20260801-120000.md`.

**S4 BUILD — ALL 10 TASKS DONE, LIVE-VERIFIED, NOTHING OUTSTANDING** (21 commits on `main`; viewer 432 → 716, renderer 676 → 724).
- **T1 migration 0020** — `section_checks` + `get_activity_version_for_check` (the authorization chain) + `record_check` (service-role only, per-student rate ceiling, idempotent replay, 0005-style attempt race backstop). Probe-verified 22/22; applied + verified live same day (12/12 — structure exact, behaviour verified impersonated in a rolled-back transaction: A2 foreign-parent version REFUSED, B1 `authenticated` CANNOT call `record_check`, anon refused, attempt numbering, idempotent replay returning ORIGINAL verdicts, rate ceiling, RLS matrix; zero probe residue; ACL completeness still exactly the documented 3.2A anon exception).
- **T2 grading engine** (`packages/viewer/src/server/grading/`) — blanks incl. consume-once interchangeable groups, MC, matching, ordering, the full graph family dispatched to graph-kit's pure scorers, the raw-document walk, and `gradeSection`. Outbound sanitize reuses S2's deep walk via a new `sanitizeInlineContent` export.
- **T7 expression guards** — bounds before the math engine compiles student input (new server-side threat class).
- **T6 mock↔real conformance** — the real engine asserted against the behavior all 22 S3 components were built on.
- **T3 golden corpus + PARITY GATE** — the ruled renderer-retirement gate. The corpus is DATA (`@activity/viewer/grading-corpus`, so it outlives the renderer); two halves execute it — the server engine, and the published page via `renderActivity()` → jsdom → `buildRefs()` → the runtime's own scorers. Both assert against the corpus's own expected column, never against each other (two engines can agree and both be wrong, and this one was ported by *reading* the runtime). **Red-green tested:** scoring empty as wrong breaks 2 cases, disabling normalization breaks exactly the 3 G1 divergence cases, restoring returns 50/50. Scoped honestly — the graph family is absent because the engine IMPORTS graph-kit's scorers, so its parity is true by construction; `CORPUS_COVERAGE` forces every `auto_gradable` registry type into a justified bucket and a guard fails if a new one appears without an entry.

**Three things the build found that the review did not:**
1. **A wire gap: `plot_ray` / `plot_segment` were ungradable.** The kit has always produced `shape` + endpoint styles, but the check wire dropped them and `InteractiveGraph` never forwarded them — and they are answer content, not decoration ("2 ≤ x < 7" ≠ "2 < x < 7"). Added as OPTIONAL fields, so **no `CHECK_WIRE_VERSION` bump**: absent means "no shape chosen", read as unanswered rather than a lucky default.
2. **Field names the plan assumed wrong.** There is no `strategy` (it's `answerType`, absent ⇒ text) and no `groupId` (an order-independent group is a maximal adjacent run of `interchangeableWithPrevious`). `ordering` has no key field at all — the authored item order IS the key, which is why the grader must read the RAW document.
3. **`ordering`'s omission rule needed a new idea.** The wire carries no `moved` flag, but the serve shuffle is seeded, so the server recomputes the permutation this student was served and reads "identical to what we served" as untouched.

**Author ruling G1 (2026-08-01) — typographic normalization.** Rulings 6.1A (a11y baseline names "server-side unicode-minus normalization") and 7.1A (exact parity; the runtime normalizes nothing) directly conflicted. Ruled: normalize a small closed set of look-alikes (unicode minus, smart quotes, exotic spaces) on BOTH sides. An input-method fix, not a pedagogy change — it never makes a wrong answer right, it stops punishing a right one for its encoding. Explicitly NOT case folding, whitespace collapsing, or NFKC.

- **T4 handler + `check-activity` Edge Function** — 39 branch pins. The authorization chain runs BEFORE the raw document is read and AS THE CALLER; the suite pins that ORDERING via port spies, not just the status, because a handler that 404s *after* reading the document would pass a status-only test and still be wrong. `verify_jwt` stays **TRUE** — deliberately not in the no-verify-jwt trio, since a check is always made by a signed-in student.
- **T9 `grading-server` bundle + CI drift guard + `pnpm deploy:check`.**
- **T8 client-side checking** — typed `CheckError` taxonomy (`stale_client` / `unauthenticated` / `rate_limited` / `offline` / `server_error` / `unknown`, each with `retryable`), so the viewer stops telling a student to retry a failure retrying cannot fix; per-section **idempotency key** kept across a retry and refreshed on a deliberate re-check (a 3-4 s cold start is long enough for a student to press Check twice, which would otherwise show the teacher two attempts for one piece of work); and the **passive stale-version banner** (role=status, student-chosen reload, never automatic — auto-reloading would discard in-flight work).

**⚠ THE BUNDLE CEILING CAUGHT A REAL LEAK ON ITS FIRST RUN.** The grading bundle came out at **8 MB** and failed the build: importing graph-kit's scorers through the package BARREL pulled in **1 MB of MathLive**, exactly what R2 said must never happen (the graded form is already ascii, so the server needs no LaTeX). Fixed by adding **`@activity/graph-kit/scorers`** — a narrow pure-scorer entry with no DOM, no JSXGraph, no MathLive. Bundle came out at **2516 KiB**, verified free of both. **Standing rule now in CLAUDE.md: anything server-side imports the scorers from `/scorers`, never the barrel.**

- **T5 check-response leak tests** — the check channel has a DIFFERENT rule from the read path (it deliberately releases solution + feedback after a check), so the scan is per-field and VALUE-based rather than key-name-based. The fully-loaded secret-bearing fixture moved to `tests/helpers/leakFixture.ts` so both channels scan the same document. **The fixture had a real ambiguity this exposed:** one sentinel stood for both answer material and releasable content, so the check suite went red on solution text it is supposed to return; they are now split (`STR` never released on any channel / `RELEASABLE` stripped from the read path but returned by a check). Red-green tested — disabling the outbound sanitizer fails it with the poisoned answers in the payload.
- **T10 `scripts/verify-check-e2e.js`** — drives the REAL student path end to end (get-activity resolve → content → check-activity), so a disagreement between the read API and the grader about ids fails it. Covers what neither the unit suites nor the SQL matrix can: a real session JWT surviving the round trip, the bytes the BROWSER receives carrying no answer key, persistence, attempt increments, idempotent replay.

**✅ `check-activity` DEPLOYED + SMOKE-VERIFIED 2026-08-01** (v3, `verify_jwt: true` — an unauthenticated POST gets 401 from Supabase's own gate before the handler runs; CORS preflight 204 echoing the Pages origin). **S4 LIVE-VERIFIED — E2E 13/13 on the deployed function** against activity `6a84c8cb` (a real 11-item section: 8 blanks + 1 MC + 2 free-text): a real check returns **200, 11 items graded**; **C2 the foreign-version parentage probe REFUSED live with 404** (the security hole the eng review's outside voice found, now proven closed above the database, not just in it); C3 unknown version 404 with the same no-oracle message; C4/C5 typed 400s; **D1 the bytes the BROWSER received carry no answer-key field**; 401 without auth.

**⚠ THE E2E CAUGHT A REAL PRODUCTION BUG THAT EVERY UNIT TEST MISSED.** `_shared/cors.ts` emits `{ error, details: { code } }`, but `httpCheckService.checkErrorFor` read `body.code` at the TOP level — so `wire_version_mismatch` never matched and a stale tab fell through to the generic un-retryable `unknown`, meaning **T8's whole `stale_client` path could never fire in production** (a student would get a dead end instead of "reload"). Root cause: the handler test's fake `CorsKit` spread `details` at the top level while the real helper nests it, so **both sides of the integration were written against the same wrong assumption and both passed their own tests**. The double now mirrors `cors.ts` exactly and a regression pin asserts the real body shape. Standing lesson: a test double that does not match the real implementation is worse than no double — it manufactures confidence.

**✅ FINDING R2 CLOSED — cold-start mitigation RULED (author, 2026-08-01): accept the latency, NO warm-ping cron.** Measured n=42 on the deployed function: **p50 855 ms, p90 1474 ms, p95 1503 ms**, min 598 ms, one 4183 ms cold-start outlier matching S2's independent 3–4 s figure. Warm p95 is within tolerance for an explicit action that already shows a pending state (2.1A); the cold start lands on ONE student per period and the classroom pattern self-mitigates. Full reasoning + what would reopen it: [DECISIONS.md](DECISIONS.md) → "Check-RPC latency".

**Two guard gaps the T8 work exposed and closed:** (1) the styles token guard only policed `--color/--state/--callout` names, so the banner's `var(--font-size-sm)` — a name from no token family we have — silently resolved to nothing and nothing failed; it now checks EVERY `--*` reference against tokens.css (red-green tested). (2) The store swallowed untyped errors from the check service, so a client-side `TypeError` was indistinguishable from the server being down; untyped failures now log.

## 2026-07-28 → 07-31 — components-as-data S2 (read API) + S3 (viewer, 22 components + route) + direct-to-Storage image upload (archived from STATE 2026-08-04)

### Direct-to-Storage image upload — applied, deployed, function deleted, verified end to end (2026-07-31)

**✅ COMPLETE 2026-07-31 — applied, deployed, `upload-image` deleted, VERIFIED END TO END AT BOTH LAYERS.** Eng-reviewed same day (0 unresolved; DECISIONS.md → "Direct-to-Storage image upload").

**Migration.** `db push` applied 0019 after a one-line history repair — the phantom `20260729131811` row (the 0018 MCP-apply duplicate, exactly as the 0018 wrinkle note predicted) was `migration repair --status reverted`'d. Remote history is a clean 0001–0019.

**Layer 1 — predicate (SQL, 8/8).** 0019's 5 footer checks (bucket public/10MB/5 types; EXACTLY one policy = the INSERT gate; predicate calls the helper + is CASE-guarded; RLS on; `can_edit_activity` grants untouched) plus the impersonated matrix: owner ALLOW; non-owner/anon DENY 42501; **non-uuid key = clean 42501, the cast never ran**; root-level + nested keys DENY; soft-deleted activity DENY; overwrite sees 0 rows. Zero probe residue; ACL completeness still exactly the documented anon exception; **advisor 18 lints — unchanged from the post-0018 baseline, so 0019 added none.**

**Layer 2 — live API (browser console, 8/8).** `scripts/verify-image-storage-e2e.js`, run signed-in against the deployed app. **T1 owner upload HTTP 200 — storage-js forwards the session JWT** (the assumption class that failed once on `functions.invoke` is now proven, not assumed). T2 tokenless public read 200 `image/png`. T3 duplicate → `409 KeyAlreadyExists`. **T4 upsert/overwrite → `403` RLS — the no-UPDATE-policy design confirmed at the API layer.** T5 `text/plain` → `415 invalid_mime_type` and T6 11 MB → `413 EntityTooLarge` (**the bucket limits are real enforcement, fired on bodies that bypassed the client guard entirely**). T7 non-owned activity → `403`. T8 malformed key → `403`, **not a 5xx** — the D1 CASE pin holds above the DB too.

**Deploy integrity verified:** live bundle `index-BCenC_5H.js` (hash-identical to a local build) carries `activity-images` and zero `upload-image`; function list down to `publish-activity` / `ingest-submission` / `get-feedback` / `get-activity`; app boots clean. **Operational note for next time:** the function was deleted before the Pages build finished, so uploads were briefly broken (old bundle → deleted function). Harmless here, but the ordering exists for a reason — wait for the deploy, then delete.

**`cacheControl` RESOLVED 2026-07-31 — our code is correct; the free tier does not pass it through.** A real editor upload (156 KB webp) stored `metadata.cacheControl = "max-age=31536000"`, proving `uploadImage.ts` sends it and supabase-js's multipart branch honors it (the Blob-vs-bytes worry from the Edge-Function era was unfounded — no code change needed). **But the public endpoint serves `cache-control: no-cache`** (`sb-gateway-mode: direct`, `cf-cache-status: REVALIDATED`): Supabase's Smart CDN, which is what passes the stored value to browsers, is **Pro-plan-and-above**. Nothing in our code can change the served header — the stored value is already right. **Measured impact is small and was verified, not assumed:** the response carries a strong `etag`, and a conditional request returns **`304` with 0 bytes**, so a revisit costs one round-trip per image, never a re-download. Accepted as-is; it becomes a free win if the project ever moves to Pro. Also worth knowing the R2 era did NOT have this limitation (raw PUT set `public, max-age=31536000, immutable` directly), so this is a real if minor regression from the migration — logged so a future perf pass doesn't rediscover it as a mystery.

**No residue.** The E2E probe object was deleted via the dashboard 2026-07-31 and verified gone (`NoSuchKey`) with the real editor upload intact (200, 156 KB, `image/webp`); the bucket holds exactly that one object. **Operational note:** deleting a bucket object requires the dashboard or service role — there is no DELETE policy, by design (it's the same absence that makes overwrites impossible), and a raw SQL delete of the `storage.objects` row is the WRONG tool: it hides the file from the dashboard without reclaiming the backing blob, foreclosing a proper cleanup. Until the orphan-GC lands (TODOS.md), dashboard deletion is the only complete path.

### The moot graph-kit upload (2026-07-31)

**~~QUEUED~~ → STRUCK 2026-07-31, provably moot — graph-kit upload + publish-activity redeploy. Do NOT run these.** Verified in code, not assumed: the viewer already loads the kit as an **app-bundled lazy Vite chunk** — `await import('@activity/graph-kit')` at four sites in `packages/viewer/src/blocks/kitSurfaces.ts` — and the editor imports it statically (`ActivityConfigDrawer.tsx`). ### S3 — the viewer (foundation V1–V8, 22 components, the live route)

**🎉 S3 VIEWER END-TO-END 2026-07-31 — the student route LIVE at `/a/:activityId` (432 viewer + 984 app tests).** Real activities load from the S2 read API and mount for a signed-in student. **Read client** (`viewer/src/client/readClient.ts`, injected transport — no supabase-js in the viewer) owns two things the route shouldn't: the **stale-version retry** (version-keyed immutable URLs mean a republish is invisible until re-resolve; the server answers 404 + `current_version_id` and `load()` follows it once) and an **error taxonomy** (`unauthenticated` / `unavailable` / `unservable` / `offline` / `unknown`) so the failure UI switches exhaustively instead of branching on status codes. **Screens:** pre-auth gate naming the activity [3.2A] → skeleton with ~8s slow-escalation + Retry [1.2A] → worksheet → one honest failure screen per kind [Q3A], with Retry offered ONLY on recoverable kinds. **`createHttpCheckService` written against the frozen wire before S4 exists** — proves the contract is implementable and gives S4 a concrete consumer. **Verified against the live deployed function** (real title rendered anonymously). **⚠ Privacy finding → the display_name pending action in STATE.**

**S3 COMPONENT LAYER DONE 2026-07-31 — 22 components + styling, 419 viewer tests.** Styling landed as ONE token-only stylesheet (`src/styles/viewer.css`) for all 22 blocks + container chrome, built to the ruled paper-worksheet direction. **Token-only is ENFORCED** (`tests/styles.test.ts`): no hex/rgb/named colour literals, shadows must be tokens, and every `--color/--state/--callout` name used must be a REAL declared token. Lengths deliberately not policed. The same suite pins the ruled floors: 44px targets, visible focus ring, reduced-motion, phone breakpoint, and print-as-BLANK-worksheet. **Two browser-only catches:** list markers had vanished (the app's reset strips ul/ol bullets; `list-style` is now explicit), and the guard's first run correctly flagged `--callout-*-print-border` as undeclared (border STYLES in `staticTokens`, not `colorTokens`).

**ALL 22 BLOCK COMPONENTS BOUND 2026-07-31 (408 viewer tests at that point).** The remaining 16 landed in one pass on the exemplar templates: 9 statics, 5 interactive, 2 containers, math_block. **~109 of the new tests were auto-generated by the conformance factory** the moment each binding landed — the D4 investment's largest dividend. Notable calls: **Ordering/Matching use buttons + a native `<select>`, deliberately NOT drag-and-drop** (drag is unusable by keyboard and hostile on touch; a drag affordance can be layered on the same state later); **FillInBlank** labels each blank "Blank 2 of 3" and renders per-BLANK verdicts; **math_block is two blocks in one** per `familyOf` — promptless = static KaTeX, gap-bearing = lazy MathLive over a KaTeX fallback; **GraphFigure stays KIT-FREE** (plain SVG). **⚠ THE TYPE SYSTEM CAUGHT A DESIGN GAP:** `Problem` cannot render an authored solution — the sanitizer strips `problem.solution` like every other, so it's server-released after the section check. **S4 CONTRACT NOTE:** `SectionCheckResult.solutions` is keyed by BLOCK id so no wire change is needed, but **the grading RPC must include solutions for STATIC blocks in a checked section**. Browser bug fixed: gap-bearing math_block rendered twice (MathLive field + a red KaTeX error, since KaTeX can't parse `\placeholder`); fallback now renders `\square` and hides once the field mounts. Browser-verified: all 22 types render in the whole-worksheet view, 32 slots, 0 crashes, 0 unbound. Server bundle held at **899 KiB**.

**CANVAS FAMILY COMPLETE 2026-07-31 — number_line + data_plot on the graph exemplar (299 viewer tests at that point).** Both copied the kit-backed pattern with no rediscovery. **number_line** needed ungraded mode in its kit widget and carries the only real geometry delta: 1-D positions ride the shared `graphs` category as `[value, 0]`, and `plot_interval`'s endpoint STYLES travel in `domain` because they're part of the answer. **data_plot** needed NO kit change — its widget already took an optional key (the registry's documented `derivableFromServed` residual); the surface still drops the client-computed `correct`. Shared bits → `canvasChrome.ts`; `graphSurface.ts` → `kitSurfaces.ts`. Browser-verified: both number-line variants + all three interactive charts mount with real handles. One browser-only bug fixed: a `minHeight` canvas leaves no DEFINITE height for the host's `height:100%`, so the kit fell back to its own default and the dotplot filled the viewport. **The conformance factory picked up both bindings automatically — 20 of the 28 new tests are ones nobody wrote.**

**S3 V9 COMPLETE 2026-07-31 — interactive_graph built, board gap CLOSED, all 6 variants browser-verified.** Chain: graph-kit ungraded mode (ruling A) → sanitizer derive step → the component → the StrictMode fix. **The empty-boards gap was a React dev-mode race, not a kit or recipe problem:** StrictMode runs mount→cleanup→mount, both async mounts shared the canvas ref element, and the kit begins with `mount.textContent=''` — whichever overlapping mount cleared LAST wiped the other's board DOM. Fix: each mount creates its own child host div, cleanup removes it synchronously (`c635204`); stress-churned 6 mount/unmount cycles — zero empty canvases, no leak. Learning logged (`strictmode-shared-container-imperative-mount`). **⚠ BUNDLE REGRESSION FOUND + FIXED (the important one):** component bindings on registry entries leaked the whole component tree into the get-activity Edge Function, because the read API imports that registry for sanitize specs — **888 KiB → 2.8 MB at V5 (missed then) → 21 MB at V9**. Bindings moved to `registry/bindings.ts` (client-only); registry.ts is pure data again; bundle back to **899 KiB**, with a size ceiling in `scripts/bundle-viewer-server.mjs` so it can't regress silently. Two more component fixes from the browser runs: a swallowed mount rejection (now an honest per-block message) and a zero-height canvas (JSXGraph needs a sized container).

**✅ RULING RESOLVED 2026-07-31 (author chose A) — graph-kit ungraded input mode BUILT.** `answerKey` optional on `mountGraphQuestion`; absent ⇒ nothing scores, no mistake classification, `correct` forced false with a new additive `scored: false` beside it so no consumer can read a verdict out of a keyless widget. **Sub-finding it exposed:** handle count and curve family were derived FROM the key but are question SHAPE, not answer — ungraded mode takes them from a new `questionShape` config, and graded mode ignores it entirely so the two paths cannot disagree. `questionRecipe` exported (the graded/ungraded fork is pure and covered by 14 tests, half pinning that GRADED behavior is unchanged). graph-kit 370→384. **Original finding, for the record:** `mountGraphQuestion` (and siblings) took `answerKey` as REQUIRED config and used it in 17 places to score client-side — but S2's sanitizer strips exactly those fields, so the kit's question widgets could not be mounted by the server-authoritative viewer. This is precisely what the kit-backed exemplar was added to surface — at block #15 it would have been expensive. Options + the recommendation are in the design doc → "OPEN RULING"; **C (serve a placeholder key) was rejected and recorded so nobody re-proposes it** (it reintroduces the exact leak the sanitizer exists to prevent). **What landed alongside, safe under every option: CHECK_WIRE_VERSION 2** — the `graphs` category (block id → geometric work), store `setGraphWork`, index routing, mock grading. It carries **work only, never `correct`**. The `unsupported` escape hatch stays live-but-empty, with a test asserting the empty roster is a claim rather than an omission.

**S2 READ API COMPLETE (2026-07-28, third session; code + tests + docs).** Lane A's first step: T2 (definer RPC + upgrade-on-read + durable per-version cache + immutable headers) + T3 (registry-driven answer-key sanitizer + wire-level leak tests) + the 3.2A anonymous meta endpoint. Eight design decisions author-approved up front; full record in [DECISIONS.md](DECISIONS.md) → "Read API S2". The shape: ONE Edge Function `get-activity` (`--no-verify-jwt`, self-verifying; anonymous meta / authed resolve / authed version-keyed content served `private, immutable`); access = any authenticated user reads the published CURRENT version (link-share behind sign-in; classes are not ACLs), enforced by `get_published_activity` running as the caller; durable cache = `activity_version_reads` DB table keyed `(version_id, sanitizer_rev)` where **`SANITIZER_REV` is computed from the registry's sanitize specs** (spec change ⇒ stale rows orphaned automatically, no hand-bump to forget); `ordering`'s serve shuffle is serve-time (seeded `version:user:block:field` — reloads never reshuffle, cache stays student-independent). New code: `packages/schema/src/upgrade.ts` (4A seam, zero migrations today, v1 fails loudly per the greenfield hard-cut), `packages/viewer/src/sanitize/` (generic SanitizeSpec transform + `sanitized-types.ts` type projection + shuffle), `packages/viewer/src/server/` bundled to committed `_shared/viewer-server.bundle.js` (CI drift-guarded — separate from the renderer bundle so the read path never loads KaTeX), `supabase/functions/get-activity/`, `supabase/migrations/0017_read_api.sql` + `scripts/verify-0017.sql`. Two hardenings beyond the S0 contract, both in DECISIONS: in-band secrets stripped by an **unconditional deep walk** (a prompted `math_inline` in ANY content array can't leak on a declaration miss), and a real S0 gap caught+fixed — **`MathPrompt.acceptableAnswers` was missing from `MATH_PROMPT_SECRET_FIELDS`** ("also accept" answers would have shipped to students; exactly the leak class the sentinel suite exists for). NOT in S2 (fence held): no grading RPC (S4), no viewer UI (S3), no publish-flow change, no census (S7).

## 2026-07-28 → 2026-07-31 — deploy/migration clearances 0015–0020 + Drop 0 hosting (archived from STATE 2026-08-02)

Completed author actions, moved out of STATE's "Pending author actions" once verified live.
**All were confirmed applied/deployed against the project on 2026-08-02** (`list_migrations`
ends at 0020; `list_edge_functions` shows get-activity v6, check-activity v3,
publish-activity v122, ingest-submission v42, get-feedback v4, flags correct) — two of these
blocks were still sitting in STATE marked QUEUED at that point, which is the failure mode
this archive exists to prevent.

**QUEUED — apply migration `0020_section_checks.sql` (S4). Probe-verified 22/22 BEFORE it was ever applied.**

```bash
supabase db push          # then: run scripts/verify-0020.sql, expect "22 PASS, 0 FAIL"
```

The migration DDL and the whole verification matrix were run together inside a **rolled-back transaction** against the live DB via MCP — 22/22 PASS, and the table, both functions, and the synthetic probe version row were all confirmed absent afterwards. So this is known-good against the real database, not just known-plausible. Two of the 22 are security boundaries rather than conveniences, and both are silent if they regress: **A2** — a version belonging to a DIFFERENT activity must be refused (without the parentage join, an empty-responses check request returns a foreign activity's entire solution set); **B1** — `authenticated` must never be able to call `record_check`, which takes verdicts as an argument and would let a student write themselves a row of `correct`.

**QUEUED — redeploy `get-activity` (now carries a real change, not just the refactor):**

```bash
pnpm deploy:get-activity
```

**Two changes ride this deploy.** (1) **NEW 2026-07-31 — the sanitizer's derive step.** Served graph blocks now carry a derived `questionShape` (handle count + curve family), without which the viewer cannot lay out a graph question at all; the answer key stays stripped, and the derived values are whitelisted to counts + a closed family enum so a coordinate cannot travel that path. `SANITIZER_REV` moved **1-5dbcb651 → 1-f8328527**, so the moment the new function is live the stale read-cache rows orphan themselves and the next read recomputes — no manual cache clear, by design. Nothing regresses meanwhile: the deployed v5 keeps serving the old rev happily. (2) The handler extraction below.

The 2026-07-29 test-setup review found the function's 374-line handler had zero automated coverage (the one S2 code gap). The handler logic now lives in the tested viewer-server bundle (`packages/viewer/src/server/get-activity-handler.ts`, 29 new branch-pin tests incl. the stale-version 404, error→status mapping, cache-fallback non-fatality, rate-limiter ceiling, and the shuffle-seed glue proven through a full request); the Deno file is thin wiring over `GetActivityDb`/`CorsKit` ports. Deployed **v5 still runs the old inline handler — identical behavior**, so nothing is broken or urgent; redeploying just aligns the live function with the repo (the pnpm script bakes in `--no-verify-jwt`). After deploy, a quick `scripts/verify-content-path.js` run re-confirms the content branch.

**DONE 2026-07-29 — S2 read API fully verified END TO END on the live deployment; nothing queued.** `get-activity` **v5, `verify_jwt:false`** (the 600/min school-safe ceiling is live); `main` pushed and in sync.

**Leak proof (the one that counts).** The first end-to-end run passed all 10 wire probes against an EMPTY activity (1 block, no answer keys) — a trivial pass, recorded as such rather than claimed as evidence. Re-run against a purpose-built fixture (`scripts/leak-test-fixture.md`, imported via the markdown importer): **22 blocks / 11,166 stored bytes** carrying 9 of 10 secret markers, served as **8,436 bytes** — ~2.7 KB stripped. Browser wire scan **clean on all 10**. Stronger check run server-side against the cached artifact itself (16 probes, incl. ones the browser list omitted): `answer`, `acceptableAnswers`, `correct`, `solution`, `rubric`, `key`, `mistakeFeedback`, `noSolutionCorrect`, `correctInterval`, `inequalities`, `models`, `regions`, `tolerance`, `partialCredit`, `builtinFeedback` **all absent**; `allowNoSolution` **present**, exactly as the registry declares (it renders the "no solution" control). **Two honest gaps, both unit-covered, neither blocking:** the fixture's graph fence produced a `graph_inequality`, so `correctPoints` had nothing to catch (that probe was vacuous), and the `ordering` serve-shuffle was not exercised in production (the cached artifact holds authored order by design; the permutation is applied per-request).

**Also verified live:** resolve `200`/`no-cache` with a real user JWT; content `200` with `private, max-age=31536000, immutable` exact; durable cache row written (`sanitizer_rev 1-5dbcb651`, `schema_version 2` — upgrade-on-read ran); stale `version_id` → `404 "Not the current version"`. Anonymous/error surface verified earlier: meta reaches our handler (proving `--no-verify-jwt`), missing-auth `401`, bad UUID `400`, `POST` `405`, CORS preflight `204` echoing the Pages origin. Ignore step-5 cache timings (1024–1281 ms) — Edge cold starts run 3–4 s, so the clock is noise; the cache ROW is the evidence.

Verified against the deployed function, not assumed: a POST carrying a gap-id key (`g`+32 hex) in `responses.blanks` — the exact shape that returned `422 responses failed schema validation / Invalid uuid` before — returned **200** with a submission id, and the row persisted with the gap key and its answer intact (validated AND stored, not just accepted). Probe row deleted afterwards. **This repaired every already-published gap-bearing page with no republish**, since the HTML always sent the right payload and only validation refused it.

**⚠️ Migration-history wrinkle (harmless, but know it):** applying via MCP recorded the version as **`20260729131811`**, not `0018`, so history and the repo filename disagree — the same divergence class that bit 0017, in the opposite direction. **This one is self-healing:** 0018's body is fully idempotent (`create or replace function`, `revoke`/`grant`, `alter policy`), so a future `supabase db push` will re-run it as a no-op and record `0018`. To keep history tidy instead, run `supabase migration repair --status applied 0018`. Either way, nothing breaks.

Behavior verified against a rolled-back fixture: resolve returns the current version; meta returns title + teacher name; meta on a draft returns 0 rows; **anon calling `get_published_activity` = "permission denied for function"**; **authenticated against a draft = "Not available"** (no oracle); `activity_version_reads` reads 0 rows as `authenticated`. ## 2026-07-27 — block-level definitions (moved from STATE's Last-updated, archived 2026-07-28)

**Block-level definitions.** A vocabulary definition now carries a `DefinitionBlock[]` instead of one flat inline run — the reference panel's content alphabet minus columns and callout — so a popover can hold a display equation, a heading, a list, an image, and a static graph figure. Author ask: "anything you can do in reference materials you should be able to do in definitions too." Design + rulings D1–D7: [docs/design/definition-rich-content.md](design/definition-rich-content.md); §9 records the four places the build corrected the design. Five slices, all on `main`, pushed. **Schema:** a curated NON-RECURSIVE union — reusing `blocks/paragraph.ts` et al. would close the cycle `DefinitionMark → block → text → mark → DefinitionMark`, and `MathBlock` is not reusable either (its `solution: InlineNode[]` is that same edge). TS7056 fired exactly as predicted and was fixed at the tightest boundary (explicit interfaces + a named union with a `z.ZodType` annotation, not five downstream annotations). Definition block ids are OPTIONAL — a required uuid would force `randomUUID()` inside the legacy upgrades and break re-serialization byte-identity. Three composing deterministic upgrades in the `Mark` preprocess (v1 string → paragraph; v2 inline array → paragraph; v2 `image` attr → trailing image block, D7), exported as `upgradeDefinitionMark` so the serializer normalizes stale Tiptap attrs identically. NO schemaVersion/wire/storage bump. **New leaf `graph-primitives.ts`** — `AxisConfig`/`EndpointStyle`/`CurveDomain`/`FunctionModel`/`DrawableColor`/`Drawable` moved out of `blocks/interactive-graph.ts` (which re-exports every one, so all existing import paths and identities are unchanged); without it, `inline.ts → graph-figure → interactive-graph → inline` would throw a TDZ ReferenceError at import time. **Renderer:** `definition-content.ts` dispatches to the SAME block renderers the body uses so markup cannot drift (only `math_block` is local — no gap/solution machinery); `blockIdAttr` omits `data-block-id` when there is none, keeping body output byte-identical. The definitions sidecar is UNCHANGED at 1.9 KiB and the base runtime at 41.8 KiB — the `<template>` seam means block content costs the runtime nothing. **Print:** `meta.print.printDefinitionGlossary` (OFF by default, unlike `printReferencePanel`) emits an end-of-worksheet glossary appendix, collected by a STRUCTURAL walk over the document rather than a typed visitor (a definition mark rides any text node — blank hints, MC choices, matching sides, nested list items — and a typed walker would silently miss the next block type added). **Editor:** popover for a simple definition, a new modal `DefinitionEditDialog` for a rich one, hard-gated by `definitionShape.ts`. The gate is a DATA-LOSS guard: the popover commits its whole draft on every exit path, so an inline editor opened on multi-block content would delete every block after the first on a stray Escape. "Simple" = optional leading paragraph + optional trailing image, which is exactly what the legacy upgrades produce, so no existing definition turns read-only. The dialog does not register the `Definition` mark, enforcing non-recursion at the editor level too. New `definitionSafe` slash flag (orthogonal to `referenceSafe` — `graph_figure` is `referenceOnly` yet belongs here). **Import:** a ` ```definitions ` fence (entries split by `---`, headed by `term:`) + `[[term]]` references, resolved case-insensitively in a genuine two-pass over markdown-it's TOKEN list so order does not matter; the reference fence's line grammar was extracted to a shared `parseContentLines`. `[[term :: text]]` is unchanged. Registered in `importFormatRegistry.ts` — this fence is a THIRD kind of side channel (it contributes blocks nowhere, reaching the document only inside a mark), so `FenceSpec` gained `definitions`/`probeTerm`; the guard failed the build until the prompt + format doc taught it. Browser-verified at every slice: rendered page popover (KaTeX + graph SVG + scroll + Escape/focus-return), the print appendix under forced `@media print`, the live editor (Define → popover → Add more… → dialog → Save → read-only popover, with Escape AND outside-click both leaving rich content byte-identical), and the real importer. Suite **2274 green**; typecheck clean; lint 0 errors. **Pushed + `publish-activity` redeployed (v120, `verify_jwt:true`) and verified 2026-07-27 — nothing queued.**
